After suffering for their accumulated sins in each hell in sequence, cast them into the mortal world to be born in low species."

Yama said, "Messengers, listen to their deeds; there is a trace of merit in them. By chance, they saw and worshipped the elephant-faced Lord. By that merit, He will eventually deliver them." Hearing these words, the messengers bound and beat them severely. They were cast into the Kumbhipaka, Shonitoda, Raurava, and Kalakuta hells for hundreds of years each. They were taken to Tamisra, Andhatamisra, the hell of pus and blood, and the hell of thorns, where their limbs were wounded, and they were scorched in hot sand. They were severely wounded by worms and needle-faced creatures, and then taken to the terrible Asipatra forest, where the bodies of sinners are torn by weapons. Then they were crushed on hot stones by heavy blows.

Suffering in twenty-one hells for many days, their pain cannot be fully described even by Shesha. After suffering such torments for thousands of years, they exhausted their sins and returned to earth. One was born as a crow, another as an owl. Then one became a frog, and the other a chameleon. Then one became a snake, and the other a scorpion. Even there, they committed sins by biting various creatures. They were born as dogs and cats, mongooses and pigs, wolves and jackals, horses and donkeys. Then they were born as camels and elephants, then as crocodiles and great fish. Then they were born as tigers and deer, then as bulls and buffaloes. Thus, going through various species, they were born as outcastes and insects. Finally, they were born as a Rakshasa and a Bhilla (tribesman). They became known on earth by the names Pingaksha and Durbuddhi.

The great extent of the sins committed from birth is told. When they were the King and minister, they were engaged in sin, but they had one merit: while wandering during the hunt, they bowed to, saw, and circumambulated Vinayaka. The elephant-faced Lord was pleased with their worship of fruits and flowers. That merit, accumulated in that birth, was profound. When that Rakshasa came to eat Bhima, he climbed a Shami tree, and its leaves fell on the head of Ganesha. The Lord was pleased with both of them. Granting them a divine body, the Lord took them to the heavenly realm. I have told you the deeds of their previous birth. The elephant-faced Lord was pleased only by the offering of Shami leaves. I will also tell you, O Sage, how this image was installed by Vamana and how the Lord of Ganas became pleased and granted boons to him.

Chapter 29 : The Slaying of Virochana

The King said: I have commanded Kashyapa to perform various kinds of creation. He was humble, highly wise, and knew the past, present, and future. Possessing the brilliance of the sun and fire, and being a treasure of great penance, he performed intense austerities to attain the power of creation. Meditating on the elephant-headed God using the six-syllable mantra, after a thousand divine years, the Lord became pleased and granted him all his desired boons. Then, whatever he conceived in his mind, he saw it manifest immediately due to the power of the boon. By offering his seed to his fourteen wives, he created the entire moving and non-moving world with his brilliance. Among them, two wives were supreme, named Diti and Aditi.

Aditi also performed penance to please Vishnu. After ten thousand years, Janardana was pleased. To protect the world, to establish righteousness, and to subdue the wicked sons of Shakra and others, He became her son, having been propitiated by her earlier. Then, Diti spoke to her husband respectfully regarding the union, saying, "O Lord, give me the union, for the fire of desire torments me." Hearing her words, the sage said to her respectfully, "This time is terrible, the time for the fire ritual has arrived. Have patience for a moment; I will enjoy with you afterwards. At this time, a wicked offspring who opposes the gods and righteousness will be born." Hearing the husband's words, she held his hand and bowed to him. With red eyes, wishing to burn the three worlds, she said, "If you do not fulfill my desire right now, O sage, I will abandon my body, being distraught by the fire of desire. Let the offspring who opposes the gods and righteousness be born to me by fate, whatever it may be." Hearing her words, the one who is affectionate to those who seek refuge granted her the union, and then, after bathing, he performed the fire ritual.

From her, a son named Hiranyaksha of great strength was born. Both he and Hiranyakashipu performed intense penance. For many years, they meditated on the five-syllable mantra of their father, standing on one toe, consuming only air, and becoming covered in anthills. Then, the compassionate Mahadeva arrived to grant a boon. They praised the three-eyed God with devotion, with folded hands and bowing, their minds illuminated by His vision as much as their knowledge allowed. Praising Mahadeva, the all-pervading Lord, the wish-fulfilling tree for devotees, they bowed to Him. Pleased, He told them to ask for a boon. They asked for a boon that they would not be killed by gods, demons, Yakshas, Rakshasas, Kinnaras, great humans, ghosts, or Charanas; nor by weapons, nor by wet or dry things, nor by aquatic creatures; nor by moving or non-moving beings; neither by day nor by night; neither on earth nor in the sky, nor in the intermediate space, nor at dawn. Saying "So be it," the Lord disappeared.

At that time, they conquered the earth and the netherworld. They occupied all the abodes of the gods by force. From Hiranyakashipu was born a son named Prahlada, who was supreme among the devotees of Vishnu and a thorn in the side of his lineage. While chanting the name of Lord Narayana, he was severely beaten by his father. He was protected in many ways from various tortures—in water, on land, from poison, and from fire—by the compassionate Vishnu. This Hiranyakashipu, who terrified all worlds, was torn apart by the nails of the Narasimha form. Previously, he was killed by the Boar (Varaha) avatar, who retrieved the earth with His tusks. Bali was the son of Virochana, and Virochana was the son of Hiranyakashipu.

Virochana, a devotee of the Sun, obtained a crown. The Sun-god told him, "When this crown is touched by another's hand, its destruction will surely happen, there is no doubt." By the boon of the Sun, he brought the three worlds under his control. The gods, having lost their positions, sought refuge in the Imperishable One (Vishnu). He, having pondered deeply, could not find a solution. Then, He assumed the form of a woman, the most beautiful of all, and with that form, He delighted Virochana for a long time. Then, the foolish Virochana became ready to engage in intimacy. The beautiful-toothed woman said to him, "I have one desire, O Lord. Perform an oil massage first, and then you shall enjoy with me." Hearing those words, he, blinded by lust, agreed. She then removed his crown and rubbed oil on his head. At that moment, he was reduced to a hundred pieces and attained the same form as the Lord.

Thus ends the thirty-first chapter of the Childhood Sports in the Krida Khanda of the Shri Ganesha Purana, titled The Killing of Virochana.

Chapter 30 : The Narrative of the Vamana Incarnation in the Childhood Deeds

Ka said: Bali, being a devotee of Hari, did not suffer from any distress. He was a knower of the Vedas and Vedangas, wise, and an expert in all scriptures. He was averse to slandering others, coveting others' wealth, or harming others. He was a donor, a performer of sacrifices, and one who honored those worthy of honor with great respect. Whether walking, speaking, sleeping, eating, standing, chanting, or drinking, he constantly meditated on Hari with a steady mind and devotion.

Once, Bali summoned Shukra, honored him according to the scriptures, and asked him about statecraft, saying, "Indra enjoys the kingdom of Amaravati, yet despite our brotherhood, why do we not have a share? O wise one, please resolve this doubt of mine completely."

Shukra replied: Your ancestors waged constant war with the gods for this very reason, and they were all destroyed. The gods, aided by Vishnu and relying on the power of various illusions, triumphed. Abandoning enmity, you should perform a hundred sacrifices. By the power of that merit, you will attain the position of Indra in its entirety.

Bali said: You have spoken well, O Brahmin, expert in the science of politics. Among the methods of conciliation, donation, and division, punishment is considered the lowest. Even that succeeds through the power of merit; therefore, merit is considered the firmest foundation.

Ka said: Having made this decision, he summoned sages like Vashistha and Bhrigu. After honoring them, he began the great sacrifice at an auspicious time, which brought joy to all. They prepared the sacrificial pits after determining the cardinal directions and arranged for all the sacrificial materials, altars, and pavilions after purifying the land. After performing the preliminary rites, the shraddha, the auspicious chanting, and the worship of the Matrikas, the Brahmins installed all the pavilion deities with various mantras.

The couple shone there, adorned in white garments, surrounded by ministers, friends, and citizens. The women, honored, watched the grand festival from the windows. The Brahmins performed the preliminary rites and, according to the Vedic injunctions, sacrificed the animal. They offered oblations to the respective deities with the appropriate mantras. In the four-doored sacrificial arena, which was open to all, people of all three types came and went, having been honored. In one place, scholars engaged in great debates; in another, Apsaras danced and Vedic scholars recited. Vaishnavas and Shaivas sang to the accompaniment of drums and cymbals. Elsewhere, Brahmins ate to their hearts' content, enjoying food of six flavors. Some told stories, while others listened with joy.

In this grand sacrifice, sages like Vashistha poured a great stream of ghee into the fires. After completing the concluding rites, they placed the couple on a chariot. All the people went out to perform the Avabhritha bath, accompanied by the sound of various instruments, bards, Vedic chants, and Hari-pleasing Sama hymns. Having bathed and returned, Bali pleased the Brahmins with heaps of jewels, wealth, various garments, cows, horses, elephants, and fragrant items, fulfilling all their desires.

In this way, he completed ninety-nine sacrifices. When the hundredth sacrifice began, Indra became deeply anxious. Fearing the loss of his position, he sought refuge in the younger one, the Lord who rests on Shesha in the middle of the Ocean of Milk. He, whose feet were massaged by him and served by the gods, was being praised with devotion by Narada, who held a veena in his hand, along with Tumburu and other Gandharvas, Apsaras, and Kinnaras. Seeing Hari, Indra bowed down, joined his palms with devotion, and prayed for the fulfillment of his desire.

Indra said: Salutations to the Infinite Power, salutations to the Source of the Universe, salutations to the Sustainer of the Universe, salutations to the Creator of the Universe. Salutations to the Destroyer of the Daityas, salutations to the One with many forms, salutations to You who always grant the desires of those whose minds are attached to You.

Ka said: Having praised Him thus, Indra told Him about Bali's actions. "Bali, the son of Virochana, famous in the three worlds, will take my position through this hundredth sacrifice." Vishnu, knowing everything that was in his mind, spoke to Indra, who was troubled by anxiety: "O Indra, Bali is my devotee, an ascetic, and a conqueror of his senses. According to the Vedic injunctions, he must enjoy the fruits of his penance. No action, whether good or bad, goes in vain. I will accomplish your task; steady your mind."

Thereafter, the Lord entered the womb of Aditi. In the ninth month, Aditi gave birth to a beautiful son. By his radiance, the lamps lost their brightness. After bathing in cool water, the wise Kashyapa performed the birth rites according to the scriptures, followed by the auspicious chanting. After performing the ancestral sacrifice and the worship of the Matrikas, he fed the child ghee and honey. He then saw the child—short in stature, with a broad head, small feet, and a large body. Knowing him to be Vishnu, endowed with divine knowledge and a divine four-armed form, adorned with various ornaments, he bowed down. With a joyful heart, he said, "Today my penance, my lineage, and my eyes are blessed. My knowledge, my hermitage, and my wealth are blessed. The earth, heaven, and the netherworld are blessed by your sight, O Being of Truth, Consciousness, and Bliss."

Ka said: Hearing those words, the Lord of the Universe, satisfied with Aditi's penance, said, "I have come as your son to remove the incomparable burden of the earth and to accomplish the task of the elder." Having said this, He became like an ordinary child, cried, and drank milk. The sage performed the naming ceremony, the first feeding of grain, the tonsure in the third year, and the sacred thread ceremony in the fifth year.

Thus ends the thirtieth chapter of the Krida Khanda in the Ganesha Purana, titled "The Narrative of the Vamana Incarnation in the Childhood Deeds."

Chapter 31 : The Childhood Deeds of Vamana

Kasha said: The sage Kashyapa taught the four Vedas along with their auxiliary branches to his son. One day, Vamana asked his father, "By what means can the gods regain their status? Please explain to me how the burden of the earth can be removed."

Kashyapa replied: "My son, I will instruct you in the six-syllable mantra that grants success and pleases the Lord of Obstacles (Ganesha). When that Lord, who governs the creation, sustenance, and dissolution of the universe, the cause of all causes, and the creator of countless universes, is pleased, all desires are fulfilled. Make an effort for that."

Kasha said: Having said this, at an auspicious moment, he gave him the great mantra. Filled with joy, the child bowed to his father, took his permission, and left to perform penance. While wandering, he saw an excellent place in Vidarbha, adorned with creepers, trees, and a beautiful lake. There, he sat in the lotus posture and chanted the sacred six-syllable mantra for a year, observing a fast and controlling his senses.

Knowing his devotion, the Lord, accompanied by Siddhi and Buddhi, appeared before him. The Lord, riding a peacock, adorned with a trunk, ten shining arms, and a garland of gems, with a navel shining like a serpent, and decorated with various ornaments, approached his steadfast devotee like a cow approaches her calf with affection. Seeing the Lord before him—the one who surpasses all brilliance, the granter of all success, and the remover of all obstacles—Vamana praised the Lord of Lords, the elephant-faced deity, with supreme devotion.

Vamana said: "I bow to the Lord of Obstacles, who is unmanifest yet the cause of the manifest, praised by the Vedas, the Lord of all gods, the ruler of universes, the cause of the world's creation, and the one known through all Vedanta. He is beyond Maya, self-revealed, the cause of sustenance and dissolution, the repository of all knowledge, the Lord of all, the form of all, the remover of all fears, the granter of desires, and possesses a beautiful form. I bow to the Lord of Obstacles, served by Brahma, Shiva, and the sages, worshipped by the Nagas, the embodiment of brilliance, the truth, free from the three qualities, and known through the 'Tat Tvam Asi' (Thou art That) philosophy. He takes a form for the sake of his devotees, brings joy to his own people, illuminates the knowledge of truth, is praised by Shiva and others, dwells in all beings, and grants both worldly enjoyment and liberation."

Kasha said: Pleased by this prayer, the Lord of Lords said to Vamana, "I am satisfied with your prayer and penance; ask for any boon that is in your mind, and I shall grant it."

Vamana replied: "Even the lotus-born Brahma and the sages like Sanaka do not know your true form. Since I have seen you, the Lord of the worlds, what other boon can I ask for today? Yet, for fear of disobeying your command, I ask for this boon: May I never face defeat in any endeavor, and may there be no fear of obstacles. Bali is prepared to seize Indra’s throne by the power of his sacrifice, and he has sought my refuge. Please act in such a way that his success is ensured, if you are pleased with me, O sinless Lord of the world."

Gajanana (Ganesha) said: "By merely remembering me, all this will happen, O virtuous one. All your tasks, whether great or small, will be accomplished."

Kasha said: After the Lord departed, Vamana had a beautiful image made of Kashmiri stone. It was four-armed, three-eyed, adorned with a trunk, and with a graceful hand granting fearlessness to devotees. It was capable of granting all desires through remembrance, sight, meditation, and worship. He had a temple built of gold and gems. He installed a Brahmin there, gave him a village and wealth, and had him perform worship three times a day.

Vamana returned home, bowed to his father, told him everything, and asked for permission to go to the sacrifice. Permitted by Kashyapa, Vamana, carrying a staff of Darbha grass, wearing a sacred thread and a deerskin, and girded with a waist-belt, arrived at the sacrifice. Seeing the short-statured sage, the other sages were amazed, thinking, "This is no ordinary sage, for his brilliance is truly wonderful."

Seeing him approach, Bali rose quickly, bowed, and asked, "Who are you? Where have you come from? What do you desire? Who are your parents, and where do you live?"

Vamana replied: "O King, I do not remember my parents. Know me as a homeless, humble person. My abode is the three worlds. If you have the power, grant me land measuring three paces to build a small hut."

Kasha said: Hearing these words and realizing the compassion in his heart, Bali prepared to grant the land. Then Shukracharya said to Bali, "This is not a Brahmin, but Hari in a deceptive form. He will take away the three worlds under the pretext of three paces. Do not give him anything; I tell you the truth."

The great demon king replied, "O fool, what are you saying? If Hari himself comes and I turn him away, all my merit will be lost. What better recipient could there be than this? Whatever is given to him is eternal." Saying this, the demon king said to Vamana, "O Brahmin, the land is granted by me; please perform the vow."

As Vamana prepared to perform the vow, Indra, who had entered another body, blocked the spout of the vessel. Vamana poked it with a straw, causing Indra to lose an eye and flee. Then, Bali poured water into Vamana's palm. Remembering Ganesha, Vamana grew in size. With one step, he covered the heaven and the earth; with the second, he covered the netherworlds; and he placed the third on Bali's head.

Then the Lord said to Bali, "Go to the netherworld." Bali replied, "How can I go without you?" Hearing this, Vamana said, "My presence will be there too, by your grace. Indra has sought my refuge, and his work is dear to me. But since you are my devotee, you too will be dear to me. When Indra's tenure ends, I will give that position to you."

Then Brahma and the other gods praised the Lord Vamana. They showered flowers, the gods played musical instruments, and others sang and danced. Then the Lord Vamana, of infinite prowess, disappeared. The gods, filled with joy, returned to their respective abodes as before.

Kasha said: Thus, the image created by him became famous in the three worlds as the granter of all desires for all people. This is the story of Bali's deeds, the humility of Vamana, and the glory of the elephant-faced Lord. I have now clearly explained why he is dear to those who worship him at the place called Adosha, where he was installed by the great soul, to the south of the famous ten-armed deity.

Thus ends the thirty-first chapter of the Balacharita in the Krida Khanda of the Shri Ganesha Purana, titled "The Prowess of Vamana."

Chapter 32 : The Greatness of the Shami Tree and the Story of Kirti

Kavi said: Listen attentively, O Brahmin, to an ancient history that Gajanana himself narrated to Priyavrata.

Gajanana said: Listen, O noble one, to the great fruit of the Shami leaf. It cannot be attained through sacrifices, charity, or millions of vows. Neither through japa (repetition of mantras) nor through worship is my satisfaction as complete as it is through the Shami leaf. By the mere mention of the word "Shami," verbal sins are destroyed; by remembering it, mental sins are destroyed; and by touching it, physical sins are destroyed. Through daily worship, meditation, and bowing to it with devotion, one attains the removal of obstacles, longevity, knowledge, and the destruction of sins. There is no doubt that one attains the fulfillment of desires and stability of mind.

The Sage asked: Who was this King Priyavrata? What was his character, and where did he live? Why did the great soul praise the virtues of the Shami to him? O Brahmin, please dispel this doubt of mine.

Kavi said: O son, regarding this, they cite an ancient dialogue between Shankara and Parvati.

Parvati asked: O Lord, knower of all, Lord of the world, why is the Shami dear to Gajanana? Tell me, if you have grace upon me.

Shankara said: Listen, O beloved, I shall tell you a story, O fair-faced one, so that you may know why the Shami is dear to Ganesha.

There was once a king named Priyavrata, who was highly intelligent, truthful, virtuous, and strictly observant of his vows. He was brilliant, generous, a knower of the essence of the Vedas and Shastras, endowed with all auspicious signs, and possessed of a mighty four-fold army. He ruled the earth, treating his subjects as his own children. He had two wives: Kirti was his first wife, and Prabha was the second. He had two ministers, Dhurta and Kushala, who were skilled in statecraft and as valiant as Kartikeya. Through their wisdom and valor, the entire earth was brought under control.

However, the King was led astray by his wife Prabha. He spent his days and nights playing with her, as she was adorned with various ornaments and possessed a youthful body more beautiful than even the Apsaras. He could not bear to be separated from her even for a moment. He always insulted his first wife, Kirti, and would not even listen to her words. He would look at her with anger and even contemplate her death. He would not speak to her himself, nor would he accept anything offered by her.

Eventually, a son was born to Prabha, who resembled his father. The King performed the birth rituals and various religious ceremonies for him. He named him Padmanabhi, as suggested by the Brahmins. The boy grew like the moon in the waxing fortnight. He was intelligent, skilled in warfare, and even surpassed his father. When the boy was five, the King performed his sacred thread ceremony. Priyavrata, the mighty King of the Madras, arranged for the daughter of the King of Panchala to be his wife. They celebrated the wedding with great splendor.

Once, the first wife, Kirti, who had been reduced to the status of a maid, came to the King to massage his feet. She fell to the ground after being kicked by her co-wife. Distressed, weeping, and ashamed, she went to her own quarters, thinking, "To whom shall I go for refuge? Who will remove my sorrow? The eternal Hari, who is the ocean of mercy, was the savior of Draupadi when she was insulted by her co-wife; He will be my savior."

She thought, "Since my husband does not value me, and no one else does either, there is no point in living. I shall wither away my body. I will drink poison or fall into a deep well." Her mind could not find peace, and she was overwhelmed with distress.

At that moment, by divine providence, Devala, the best of Brahmins and the royal priest, arrived. He spoke to her about the worship of Gajanana, which destroys suffering and grants all desires. She immediately took an image of Gajanana made of Mandara wood. After bathing on an auspicious day, she sat down to worship Him with supreme concentration.

She worshipped Gajanana, the God of gods, first, as He is worshipped by all—Shiva, Hari, and the Mother. When one considers it, there is no difference between the five deities; the person who sees them as different goes to hell. The one Supreme Lord, full of supreme bliss, has become five-fold by His own will for the sake of the world's welfare, just as one man is addressed as son, maternal uncle, and so on.

She worshipped Him properly with sixteen offerings, Durva grass, flowers, gifts, and many salutations. Joining her palms, she praised the Lord of the world.

Kirti said: You are the foundation of the world, You are the cause of all. You are Brahma, Vishnu, and the great Sun. You are the Moon, Yama, Kubera, Varuna, and Vayu. You are the oceans, the rivers, and the collection of creepers and flowers. You are happiness, sorrow, and the cause of both; You are destruction and the liberator. You are the one who creates obstacles for the wicked and the one who creates great obstacles; You are the Virat. You are the giver of sons and wealth, You are the fulfiller of all desires. You are the source of the universe and the destroyer of the universe. You are Prakriti, and You are the great, attributeless Purusha. You are the one who nourishes the entire world in the form of the Moon. You are the past, the present, and the future, the self-effulgent one. You are the refuge of all beings, the destroyer of enemies, the supreme one. You are devoted to ritual, the performer of sacrifices, devoted to the path of knowledge, and pure. You are the knower of all, the ordainer of all, the witness of all, and the all-pervading one. You are the all-pervading Vishnu, the Lord who is the embodiment of all truth. You are the embodiment of all Maya, and the knower of all Mantra and Tantra rituals.

Chapter 33 : The Reviving of the Child

Kritayuga said: Thus, she worshipped and praised Lord Vinayaka every day. One day, her companions went in search of Durva grass. Due to the season of Shukra, they could not find any fresh Durva sprouts anywhere. Instead, they brought many Shami leaves to her. They returned exhausted and informed her about the unavailability of Durva. She then worshipped the Lord with Shami leaves, performing all the rituals. Without Durva, she remained fasting, absorbed in her vows. The Lord was immensely pleased by the offering of Shami leaves. She slept before Him and had a wonderful dream. At the end of the year, the same idol spoke to her: "I am very pleased with the Shami leaves, and I grant you a boon, O beautiful one." When she did not say anything, Kirti spoke to the idol again.

The Idol said: Your husband will be devoted to you, and you will enjoy many auspicious pleasures. You will have a son who will be devoted to you and possess great strength. Name this beautiful child 'Kshipraprasada'. In his fourth year, he will die from poison, but in an instant, the sage Gritsamada will bring him back to life. He will become a long-lived king and a righteous ruler. Whoever recites this hymn, which is the cause of my satisfaction, will have kings under his control, let alone ordinary people. He will be blessed with sons, wealth, and mastery over the Vedas and Vedangas; his intellect and memory will grow strong. Whoever recites this three times a day will obtain his desired objects, and by my firm devotion, he will attain liberation at the end.

Having granted these boons in the dream, the deity vanished in an instant. She awoke with tearful eyes, amazed, and thought in her mind: "The Lord of the helpless has bestowed great grace upon me. It is a great blessing that He gave me His vision. I have been purified by the Lord Vinayaka, who is compassionate to all beings. This is the ultimate perfection, the fruit of my penance, and the greatest gain that He spoke to me."

Kritayuga said: Then, at dawn, she bathed, filled with supreme joy, and worshipped the auspicious Ganesha, the giver of boons, with great devotion. After completing her vow, she placed the idol back in its original spot. She brought a priest and worshipped with devotion, meditating on Ganesha and reciting His names. Remembering the boons and waiting for the right time, Kirti lived on. After a long time, Prabha lost her radiance. She became afflicted with leprosy due to her past karma and the will of God; her hands, feet, and nose began to decay daily. Her husband, seeing her in such a repulsive state, abandoned her in his heart. He stopped speaking to her and looking at her, knowing all his efforts were in vain. Then, he went to Kirti’s house, who was beautiful due to her penance. By the grace of the Lord of Ganas, he became devoted to her. He took her by the hand and brought her to his palace. He played with Kirti as he wished, becoming completely devoted to her. The king could not bear to be separated from her even for a moment. She, being a virtuous woman, enjoyed endless pleasures and ornaments. In time, she became pregnant and gave birth to a son. At an auspicious moment, the king gave many gifts. Rejoicing at the birth of his son, he gave alms to the Brahmins as appropriate, placed five Brahmins in the directions, and performed the birth rituals. He named the child 'Kshipraprasada'. He was delighted to see his son adorned with various ornaments. Kirti was filled with great love upon seeing her son, while Prabha, seeing the beautiful son of her co-wife, became miserable. Thinking, "He will be the king and the eldest son," she gave poisoned rice to Kirti's son. He became distressed, his eyes rolled back, and he died in an instant. Prabha cried out loudly in great agony. Hearing her cry, Kirti went to him and respectfully informed her husband about the incident. The physicians, by the king's order, gave medicines, but after examining the poison, they informed the king. No mantras or medicines were effective. Prabha was rebuked by her husband and cast far away from the house. Kirti took the child and went to the forest with her companions. A yojana away from the city, the child died. Holding the child in her lap, she wept, overwhelmed by grief. She fell to the ground unconscious like a vine blown by the wind. Her ornaments fell off, her bracelets broke, and her veil was disheveled. After a moment, regaining some composure, she remembered the Elephant-faced Lord. Remembering the child's past actions, she struck her heart. By destiny, the great sage Gritsamada passed by that path, radiant like another sun. He was a foremost devotee of Ganesha and a great reservoir of penance. He stood there, filled with compassion. Kirti bowed to him and, gasping for breath, said, "This child of mine is dead; bring him to life by the power of your penance. By the command of the gods, he was named Kshipraprasada. Due to the evil nature of my co-wife, poison was given to him. O sage, he has met such a death. The sight of the virtuous is never in vain; I beg you, for the righteous do not abandon those who seek refuge."

Hearing her words, the great sage remained in meditation for a moment and then said: "Listen, O Devi, I will tell you the way to revive your son. You worshipped Vinayaka with Shami leaves out of ignorance. Offer the merit of that worship to him by my command. By that grace, your son will rise immediately." Kirti, filled with joy at the end of his words, offered the immense merit born of the Shami worship to the child. The delicate boy rose as if sprinkled with nectar. She bowed her head to the sage's feet again in joy. She asked the sage, "How did the worship with Shami leaves come to be? Tell me the glory of Shami worship, by which the child was revived and freed from the messengers of Yama. Having known this, I will worship with Shami leaves daily. By this, the cruel and formidable messengers of Yama were repelled by the messengers of the Lord of Ganas on the battlefield. Having revived my child, they went to the abode of the Elephant-faced Lord. By what merit did my child leave the poison and become happy again, rising quickly as if awakened from sleep? Therefore, I ask about the glory of the Shami leaves."

Kritayuga said: Hearing her words, Gritsamada spoke to her.

Gritsamada said: Who on earth can fully describe the glory of the name, the mere utterance of which destroys crores of sins? I will tell you the glory briefly according to my understanding. The fruit obtained from vows, charity, penance, visiting various holy places, practicing the five fires, living in water, or enduring the cold, is not equal to the merit of worshipping with Shami leaves. Whoever remembers, bows to, or worships the Shami tree daily in the morning or at the three junctures with devotion, meditating on the Elephant-faced Lord, Vinayaka is pleased with him. He grants desired objects and, in the end, liberation without a doubt. In this regard, they cite the ancient history of the dialogue between Narada and the great Indra.

Thus ends the thirty-third chapter named 'The Reviving of the Child' in the Krida Khanda of the Shri Ganesha Purana.

Chapter 34 : The Story of Aurava and Shaunaka

Kirti said: O Brahmin, please tell me the entire dialogue between Narada and Indra. Having heard it, we shall be satisfied as if by nectar, and all our doubts will be dispelled.

Ka said: Having heard her request, the sage began to narrate the history and the dialogue between Narada and Indra.

The Sage said: O beautiful one, once, while wandering and engaged in traveling through the three worlds, Narada, who possesses divine vision, reached the abode of Indra by chance. Indra worshipped him and asked about the deeds of Aurva. Listen, O great sage, to what the wise Narada said.

Narada said: In the land known as Malava, there lived a Brahmin named Aurva, who was a master of the Vedas and Vedangas, shining like the sun without any eclipse. He was capable of creating, protecting, and destroying the universe by his mere will. He was always devoted to his virtuous wife and viewed a lump of clay, a stone, and gold with equal indifference. He was wise and supreme in penance, like another Agni. His wife, Sumedha, was extremely righteous. She was a wave of beauty, adorned with various ornaments, and her beauty surpassed that of Rati and put the celestial nymphs to shame. She was devoted to serving her husband and was cherished by him with great respect. They had a daughter, whom they both cherished dearly. They named her Shamika according to their own wishes. Whatever she requested, her father, who was powerful, would grant her.

When the beautiful girl turned seven, Aurva began to think about a husband for her. He heard of the son of Dhaumya, a master of the Vedas and scriptures, a disciple of the sage Shaunaka, and a radiant, supreme sage. He was devoted to his guru’s words, self-controlled, and served his teacher well. His name was Mandara, and he was peaceful. Aurva invited him on an auspicious day and gave her to him according to the rites, bestowing many gifts. After the wedding, Mandara returned to his own ashram.

Knowing that she had reached youth, Mandara returned for Shamika. He was honored and worshipped with great respect. After feeding him at an auspicious time and giving him clothes and gold, the Brahmin sent them both off and said to his son-in-law: "O Brahmin, this daughter of mine has been given to you according to the rites. Protect her with great affection, just as I have done until today." Bowing down, the son-in-law said "Om" to his father-in-law and departed. Reaching his own ashram, he lived happily with his wife.

Once, the chief of sages, Bhrushundi, who was a devotee of the elephant-faced Lord (Ganesha), came to Mandara’s ashram. He was like fire—angry if provoked, but like the Lord if pleased. Through his penance, his trunk had emerged, and thus he became known as Bhrushundi. He had a large belly, a massive body, and was adorned with various ornaments. Mandara and Shamika both saw him. Seeing him looking deformed, they laughed with joy. Terrified by the fear of insult, he became angry, his eyes turning red. He said to them, "O dull-witted ones, blinded by pride, you do not know whom you are laughing at with your wife. Because you laughed at me, go into the form of trees, devoid of all living beings."

Ka said: Hearing the harsh curse, they both became distressed. Bowing down, they asked the Brahmin to revoke the curse. Then Bhrushundi, filled with compassion, said: "Because you laughed at my trunk out of foolishness, when the Lord of Lords, who possesses a trunk, is pleased, you will regain your own forms. There is no doubt about this." Having said this, the sage left for his own ashram.

As soon as he left, they both abandoned their human bodies and turned into trees. Mandara immediately became a Mandara tree, and Shamika became a Shamika tree, covered with thorns on all sides. Both trees became devoid of all living beings due to the sage's words.

Realizing they had not returned, Shaunaka became worried. "A month has passed; why has the powerful Mandara not returned? I will go to see him, and my disciples shall accompany me." Quickly arriving at Aurva’s place, he asked slowly: "Mandara came to take Shamika; tell me where he is."

Aurva said: "I sent them off long ago after giving him my daughter. If he has not reached his ashram, I do not know where he has gone." Then Aurva, Shaunaka, and the others began to worry. "Have they both been eaten by wolves, tigers, or hyenas on the way? Or were they killed by thieves or bitten by a venomous snake?"

Then they hurried off to find out the truth. Here and there, people said they had left a month ago. Then, in the forest, they saw the beautiful forms of a man and a woman. Realizing they were in a desolate place, they bathed, meditated, and looked. They realized that Bhrushundi had cursed them due to the anger caused by their mockery, turning them into trees devoid of all birds and insects. Mandara became a Mandara tree and Shamika a Shamika tree. Both Brahmins, Aurva and Shaunaka, grieved. "This son of Dhaumya was a virtuous man. How could he, who had studied all the sciences, be forced into the state of a tree? If his father hears this, he will give up his life. What shall I tell him when asked?" Aurva also grieved for his daughter, Shamika.

Ka said: Thinking about them both, and knowing there is no difference between the devotee and the Lord, they decided to worship Ganesha to free them from this misfortune. With controlled senses, eyes fixed upward, fasting, and firm in their vows, they stood on one leg on the earth and pleased Him with joy. Then, filled with grace, they performed supreme penance, chanting the six-syllable mantra to the Lord of Lords, Vinayaka. Thus, they performed excellent penance for twelve years—Aurva for his daughter and Shaunaka for his disciple.

Thus ends the thirty-fourth chapter of the Krida Khanda of the Shri Ganesha Purana, entitled "The Description of the Curse of Bhrushundi."

Chapter 35 : The Praise of Shami and Mandara Trees in the Childhood Pastimes

Kritayuga said: Then, pleased to see the suffering of those two, the Lord who holds the noose, the great-effulgent Vinayaka, appeared with ten arms. He wore a crown, earrings, a garland, armlets, a waist-girdle, and a snake as his sacred thread. He was mounted on a lion and shone like fire. Seeing that supreme form, which possessed the brilliance of ten million suns, both of them bowed down with folded hands and praised the Lord.

They said: You are the seed of the universe, the protector of the supreme, and the source of various joys for your devotees. You are the remover of obstacles and the fulfiller of great tasks for those whose hearts are devoted to worshipping you. You are beyond the beyond, the embodiment of the ultimate truth, known through Vedanta, and hidden deep within the heart. You are beyond the reach of all the Vedas; thus, we bow to you, our own deity. Neither Brahma, nor Shiva, nor Vishnu, nor the six-faced Kartikeya, nor the thousand-headed Shesha can know your true form, O master of Maya; how then can it be determined? When your great grace descends upon one who is experiencing the fruits of their past deeds, and they bow to you in body, speech, and mind, that person is said to be liberated while still alive. You are pleased by devotion and fulfill all desires in various forms. You are the cause of liberation from the ocean of worldly existence, and therefore, I take refuge in you, the all-pervading Lord.

Ganesha said: I am pleased by this supreme devotion, this great penance, and this excellent hymn. O Brahmins, ask for your boons. Whoever reads this hymn of mine, which destroys the misfortune of birth, three times a day, three times each, shall attain all their desires. By chanting it daily, one gains knowledge and wealth within six months; by chanting it five times, a mortal attains health and longevity.

Kritayuga said: Hearing the words of Ganesha, they spoke with great reverence. O Lord, the auspicious Shami is the daughter of Aurva. She was given in marriage to Mandara, who is learned in the Vedas and Shastras, a disciple of Shaunaka, and the wise son of Dhaumya. Once, seeing Bhrushundi on the road, they both laughed out of ignorance. Bhrushundi, considering it an insult to himself, cursed them in great anger. Due to that curse, they both turned into trees—Mandara and Shami. Their parents are grieving and deeply distressed. We are also troubled, O Lord; please do something for us all. Quickly remove the state of being trees from them, O Gajanana.

Gajanana said: How can I make the words of a Brahmin useless? I will grant a boon that fulfills the devotee's request while honoring the curse. From today, I shall remain motionless at the root of the Mandara tree. It will be honored in both the mortal world and the heavenly world. Whoever makes an image of me and worships it at the root of the Mandara tree with Shami leaves and Durva grass—these three are rare on earth—shall attain everything. Since I will always reside in the Shami tree, O sages, this boon is granted to both trees, which is very rare. According to your request, the words of Bhrushundi will not be in vain; in the absence of Durva, Mandara is accepted, and in the absence of both, Shami is considered. Both will grant the fruit; there is no need for doubt here. The merit that one attains through various sacrifices, pilgrimages, vows, charities, and disciplines, a person will attain by worshipping me with Shami leaves, O best of Brahmins. I am not as pleased by heaps of gold, gifts of food, clothes, various flowers, jewels, or pearls as I am by the worship performed by Brahmins with clusters of Shami leaves and Mandara flowers at all times, O great sages. Whoever rises in the morning, sees, bows to, and worships the Shami tree will not face distress, disease, obstacles, or bondage. By my grace, they will attain spouses, children, wealth, cattle, other desires, and finally, liberation in me. This same fruit is promised by worshipping with Mandara. By worshipping my image under these trees, I become present in their homes. Neither poverty, nor obstacles, nor untimely death, nor fever, nor fear of fire or thieves will ever occur there. A Brahmin learned in the Vedas will become wise, a Kshatriya will be victorious, a Vaishya will attain growth, and a Shudra will attain a good destination.

Kritayuga said: Having said this, the Lord stood at the root of the Mandara tree, and the Lord of Lords, Vinayaka, also stood at the root of the Shami tree. Aurva, along with his wife, remained there to perform penance. After performing penance at its root and leaving his body, he went to heaven. Aurva, through firm yoga, entered the womb of the Shami tree due to his sorrow; he became known as 'Shamigarbha' and was born from the fire-consumer (Agni). That is why those who maintain the sacred fire churn the Shami wood. Shaunaka, too, hearing the words spoken by Gajanana, made a beautiful image of Gajanana at the root of the Mandara tree and worshipped it with joy using Mandara flowers, Shami leaves, and Durva. Gajanana, being very pleased, granted boons to Shaunaka. Then he went to his own ashram and worshipped Him always.

Gritsamada said: From that time on, the Shami tree became dear to Ganesha. Thus, I have told you everything, and I shall tell you more.

Chapter 36 : The Bestowal of Boons by the Celestial Maidens

Gritsamada said: The Lord, accompanied by the daughter of the mountain (Parvati), resided on the Sahyadri mountain, surrounded by his attendants and sages in that holy and powerful region. Brahma, the grandfather of the world, desiring to see him, went there accompanied by his two wives, along with the gods, Gandharvas, and Kinnaras. Having seen the venerable Shankara, he expressed his intention. Then, Sadashiva summoned the great sages: Jamadagni, Vashistha, Markandeya, Narada, Kapila, Pulaha, Kanva, Vishvamitra, Trita, and Dvita.

Summoned by Shiva, they all arrived, as did others who wished to witness the event. At the break of dawn, they performed the auspicious rites and began the sacrifice. In their haste, they forgot the auspicious worship of Vinayaka (Ganesha). Leaving Savitri, who was occupied with household duties, the great sages sat Gayatri down, performed the purification rites, and completed the preliminary worship of the ancestors and the deities. They established the sacrificial fire, but as soon as the ritual began, Savitri arrived. Seeing the commencement of the sacrifice, she became enraged. Looking at all the assembly, she spoke with eyes reddened by anger.

Savitri said: This sacrifice, begun without honoring me, shall not succeed.

The Rishi said: Emitting flames from her mouth, desiring to burn the moving and unmoving, she cursed the gods and sages, saying, "You shall become inert." Because they had engaged Gayatri, who was unauthorized for this, all the gods, filled with anxiety, bowed down and prayed to Savitri, the mother of all. They said, "O auspicious one, due to the union of 'da' and 'la', we shall become water in the form of rivers."

Once she agreed, they took the form of rivers. Maheshwara became the Vena river, and Krishna became the Krishna river. All the gods became rivers bearing their respective names. Wherever the Lord of Obstacles (Ganesha) is not worshipped by those weak in wisdom, obstacles arise, just as they did for Shankara in the past when he destroyed the three cities due to failing to worship Ganesha first.

Brahma became deeply worried when the obstacle to the sacrifice arose. He thought, "Because of me, all the gods have turned into rivers. By what action has the chariot been moved across all worlds? How has this insurmountable obstacle to my resolve occurred today? The remover of obstacles, the Lord of the World, was neither remembered nor worshipped, which is why delusion has overcome all the gods. I will make him pleased, and then the sacrifice will proceed."

While Brahma was thinking this, the celestial women, realizing the state of the gods, approached Brahma. Pulomaja, Girija, Chhaya, Kamala, and others spoke to the lotus-faced creator, "Why was the sacrifice begun, and why were we not honored? Why was the remover of obstacles, the elephant-faced one, not worshipped first? Why was there forgetfulness in you, and why did the gods not remember him? What should we, or the gods who have become water, do? To whom shall we go for refuge, O lotus-born one, except you?"

Hearing their words, Brahma said, "Do not fear. I will perform the sacrifice such that everything becomes auspicious. What is impossible for devotees when the elephant-faced one is pleased? You all offer him devotion, and he will do what is dear to you all. I, too, shall propitiate the Lord of the World, Vinayaka, the beginningless and endless God, the cause of all causes."

Having said this, they went to a distant, auspicious land in Karnataka. Instructed to meditate on Vighnesha at the root of a Mandara tree, they went there. This was the place where Rama had previously installed the deity named Vakratunda, by whose blessings Rama was victorious, having killed the great demons and the famous ten-headed Ravana, before returning to his own abode with his wife.

All those celestial women performed severe penance there. Some chanted his name, some chanted his mantra, and some sat in the lotus posture, meditating on the Supreme Lord. Others remained in the heroic posture, fasting, while others swept the doorways, courtyards, and houses. Performing circumambulations and salutations, they practiced supreme devotion. Some stood on one toe, meditating on Vinayaka. Others closed their eyes and recited hymns.

After a long time had passed, Vinayaka was not pleased. Then, they became deeply worried, distraught, wondering what to do. Then, some offered heaps of flowers and Durva grass, while others worshipped the deity with incense, lamps, food, and gold. Some offered Mandara flowers, and others offered Shami leaves. Having heard a voice from the sky that the Lord would not be pleased without Shami leaves, they all worshipped the elephant-faced God of gods with Shami leaves with supreme devotion.

Then, out of supreme compassion, pleased by the Shami leaves, he appeared before them. He was radiant, possessing four beautiful arms, adorned with a crown, armlets, a necklace, and earrings. He had long ears, shining cheeks, was adorned with two tusks, accompanied by Siddhi and Buddhi, riding a mouse, and wearing yellow garments. All of them beheld the God, who was as radiant as a crore of suns. Closing their eyes, they bowed down like a staff on the earth. With palms joined, they praised him, their faces beaming with joy, filled with supreme bliss.

Chapter 37 : The Greatness of the Shami and Mandara Trees

Kirti said: O Great Sage, tell me how they offered their prayers to Him.

The Sage said: Listen, O Kirti, and pay close attention to that prayer.

They said: Salutations to You, who are the embodiment of all, and salutations to You, the indweller of all. Salutations to You, the creator of all, the eternal giver, and the compassionate one. Salutations to You, the destroyer of all, salutations to You, the possessor of infinite power. Salutations to You, the awakener of all, the vessel of all, and the origin of everything. Salutations to You, the Supreme Brahman, the attributeless one. Salutations to You, the embodiment of consciousness and bliss, who are beyond the reach of the Vedas. Salutations to You, the refuge of Maya, the immeasurable one, and the one beyond the Gunas. Salutations to You, who are Truth and the form of untruth, and the one who stirs the Gunas. Salutations to You, the protector of those who seek refuge, the slayer of demons, and the one who takes many forms, intent on protecting the world. Salutations to You, who hold many weapons in Your hands, the destroyer of all enemies, and the bestower of many boons, the benefactor of Your devotees.

Kritayuga said: Having prayed thus and bowed down, they asked for many boons. O Lord, grant us the forms of the deities who are of the nature of water. May we never forget You; please grant us this.

Ganesha said: Pleased by your devotion and this prayer, I grant you what you desire. So be it. By the words of Savitri, the auspicious deities who had become water were born. Brahma could not undo those words. They will return to their own forms while remaining in part as they are. They will attain their rightful positions; my words shall not be otherwise. You shall worship Me here with Shami leaves. Whoever offers a Shami leaf offers the entire world to Me. There is no doubt that they attain the fruit of a hundred loads of gold.

Kritayuga said: As Ganesha spoke thus, the deities, assuming their forms, stood there in their river-like aspects and beheld Vinayaka. They prayed to Him, bowing down to the elephant-faced Lord, saying: Forgive our offense, O Lord, which arose from the delusion of our intellect. Having abandoned You, our eldest, we began the sacrifice. We have seen the fruit of that immediately; by Your grace, we are renewed. We have attained supreme bliss through Your mercy. Having said this, they worshipped Vinayaka with Shami leaves. He, the destroyer of obstacles, acknowledged them and then vanished. Then, they fashioned a beautiful and auspicious image from stone, four-armed and elephant-faced, named Heramba. Having built a magnificent temple, they installed Him with reverence.

The deities said for the benefit of all worlds: Whoever worships this Lord of Wisdom in this city with devotion, the Lord will be pleased and grant all their desires. There is no doubt that this happens through prayer, remembrance, or bowing to Him. Indra took the great image that was made of Mandara wood to his own prosperous city. Even now, the Lord, along with his consort, worships it with devotion. All the deities, having returned to their own realms, made an image of Ganesha from the Mandara tree and worshipped it with Shami leaves. They attained joy and received supreme boons along with their spouses. Brahma, after performing great penance for twelve years, pleased Ganesha and performed the sacrifice again. He also worshipped the Mandara image of the Lord of Obstacles with great reverence, using Shami leaves in many ways, as well as gold leaves, Durva grass, Mandara flowers, Ketaki flowers, and white Durva sprouts, all of which grant the fruits of all desires.

Gritsamada said: Thus, O auspicious mother, I have described to you a portion of the glory of the Mandara and Shami, which destroys sins upon hearing. From that time on, the auspicious Shami became very dear to the Lord. Because you worshipped Him out of ignorance, this son was born. I have also properly described the glory of the Mandara. Now, give me leave, and I shall go to my own abode.

Kritayuga said: Hearing of the glory born of the Shami and Mandara, she bowed to the sage and sent forth her son to life. Whoever hears this excellent narrative, which increases the love of Ganesha, will not encounter distress and will attain all desires. Whoever rises in the morning and remembers the Shami, the Mandara, and Ganesha with devotion, will become sinless and happy.

Thus ends the thirty-seventh chapter of the Childhood Deeds in the Krida Khanda of the glorious Ganesha Purana, titled The Glory of Shami and Mandara.

Chapter 38 : The Childhood Deeds

Kirti said: O Brahmin, you have spoken well and with great respect about the glory of the Shami tree, and what you have said about the Mandara tree has also pleased my mind. This boy has been saved and instructed in the six-syllable mantra, but because he is still a child, O great sage, he is unable to chant it properly. Please instruct him now in a mantra that is easy, brings great grace, grants the kingdom of the self, and can be chanted by a child.

The Sage said: Your intellect is well-focused on the lotus feet of the Elephant-faced Lord. Therefore, I shall give him my own mantra, which bestows happiness. It is the four-syllable name that is the cause of the entire universe, the witness of all good and evil, and the destroyer of wicked demons. It is the mantra of the one who protects all dharma, who benefits Divodasa, who dwells in Varanasi in the form of a Brahmin, and who strives for the attainment of Avimukta. It is the mantra of the one who is the indweller of all, praised by Vishveshvara, worshipped by Janardana for the protection of the world, and meditated upon and worshipped by Brahma for the sake of creation. It is the mantra worshipped by Shiva for the sake of universal dissolution, and worshipped with great devotion by the husband of Pulomaja (Indra). It is the mantra worshipped by the Sun, Varuna, the Moon, Yama, Agni, and Vayu for the enhancement of their own qualities, and worshipped by Brihaspati and Ushana (Shukracharya) for their own prosperity. Dhundhi is worshipped by Shesha for the sake of supporting the earth. Gandharvas, Kinnaras, Yakshas, Siddhas, Charanas, Rakshasas, sages, animals, and all moving and non-moving beings worship the Lord of the Universe (Vishveshvara) for the fulfillment of their own tasks. This Dhundhiraja, the Lord of all, possesses infinite qualities. Even Brahma, Vishnu, Shiva, and others have not reached the end of his virtues. If one worships him with devotion, using a Shami leaf with a pure heart, your son will become a ruler of the kingdom and a destroyer of enemies.

Kirti said: O Brahmin, I have heard the entire glory of Dhundhiraja. What are his deeds, how was he born, whose portion is he, and what is his prowess? Who gave him his name, and who worshipped him in the past? O Brahmin, you should completely remove this doubt of mine.

The Sage said: You have asked well, O virtuous lady, due to your superior wisdom. Since you have asked with devotion, I shall dispel your doubt. I will tell you how the demon Durasada was killed by him, how he took a deceptive form to delude King Divodasa, how he brought Lord Vishveshvara to Avimukta, and how he came to be named Dhundhiraja in the king's palace—all of this I shall tell you just as I heard it from Skanda. Listen with a focused mind, O virtuous one, as I tell this to Agastya.

Skanda said: Listen attentively, O Brahmin, to the story of Avimukta, by the mere hearing of which one is freed from all sins. When Hari told Kashyapa to create various kinds of beings, he created the viviparous, oviparous, and sweat-born creatures, each numbering twenty-one thousand. The same number of plant-born beings were created, but among all these, human birth is rare. One attains Brahminhood through great merit. If that is well-protected by the order of dharma and adharma, it grants the supreme abode from which one never returns. Otherwise, one falls into eighty-four lakhs of births. Wicked people fall into the torments of Yama. When their karma is exhausted after a long time, they return to the mortal world as one-eyed, hunchbacked, or poor. Therefore, to have compassion on them, the gods like Brahma and Ishana, and the great sages, created various holy places. They created highly meritorious regions that destroy the sins of living beings. The gods and sages remained there, dissolving the sins of those who arrived. To protect beings from sin, the gods and sages always remained there. Vishveshvara then created the most excellent, all-virtuous, auspicious city named Varanasi, which grants both worldly enjoyment and liberation, and bestows liberation upon those who meditate on and know him. There, the powerful Vishnu created a famous lake. By bathing in it, one attains liberation. When a jewel fell from Shiva's earring due to the shaking of a cave, it became known as Manikarnika. Then, the Bhagirathi river was brought down by Bhagiratha. Here, even insects and moths attain liberation upon death. By remembering the name three times, one attains the fruit of living in Kashi. Blessed are the creepers, grasses, and trees there; they do not go anywhere else. Fish and turtles leave the earth and go to Kailasa. Shiva, the Lord of Gauri, always resides there in various forms—as the Sun, the Moon, Hari, and Brahma. He is Vinayaka, the form of Shakti, and the cause of the world. Even during the dissolution, Kashi is held on the tip of his trident. He has become fivefold for the sake of the world's grace. Whichever form the devotee meditates upon, he assumes that form in an instant. One who perceives difference here attains the hell of Avichi. One who praises him in one place but slanders him in another goes to twenty-one hells for many years. Therefore, the son of Bhasmasura, known as Durasada, approached Shukracharya and learned the five-syllable mantra. For a thousand divine years, he stood on one toe. Without food, he became like a piece of wood, covered in anthills, with only his bones remaining. With only his eyes left, he chanted the supreme mantra. Pleased by his penance, Shiva came to grant him a boon. He appeared with ten arms, five faces, adorned with a garland of skulls, holding a trident and a drum, adorned with matted hair, the bearer of the Ganges, three-eyed, smeared with funeral ashes, riding a bull, adorned with the crescent moon, and accompanied by Aparna (Parvati). He spoke to the great demon.

Shiva said: Arise, arise, may you be well. You have been greatly distressed by your penance. I shall grant you your desired boon; choose what is in your heart.

The Demon said: Then, opening his eyes and folding his hands, the demon bowed and praised the boon-giving Maheshvara. You are the cause of the world, O God, the embodiment of supreme bliss. Your form is beyond the perishable and imperishable, the creator of the three qualities. You are beyond qualities, the embodiment of knowledge, the knower of the manifest and unmanifest. Your form is meditated upon by sages, and you are the granter of grace to all devotees. You are the cause of infinite universes, the benefactor of the virtuous. You are complete, eternal bliss, and the granter of unfailing fruits. You are the support of all, the bearer of all, empowered with all strength. You are the embodiment of earth, air, water, fire, and light. Today, my penance, my eyes, my parents, and my birth are blessed, for I have seen your form directly, and my sins have been destroyed. You are the one who is never within the reach of the hearts of yogis or the words of the Vedas. Now, O God, grant me the boon I desire.

Shiva said: Fearlessly ask for what you want; I shall grant you everything, even that which is very difficult to obtain, as I am pleased by your praise, penance, and devotion.

The Sage said: Then he asked the ever-gracious Sadashiva for fearlessness from all beings born of the four sources. May I not die by the hands of Yakshas, Rakshasas, Pishachas, gods, Danavas, Kinnaras, sages, humans, Gandharvas, serpents, or forest-dwellers. May they tremble upon seeing my form in battle. O great one, grant me the kingdom of the gods, and may I have constant, unwavering devotion to your feet.

Shiva said: Except for one power, fearlessness is granted against all gods. Someone born from the power of Shakti will defeat you and then revive you. He will place his foot on your head and hold it constantly. When you are cast out by him, and his foot is on your head, then you will forcibly conquer the three worlds by your power. My devotion will be firm in you, and you will have my remembrance.

Gritsamada said: Having granted him these boons, Shiva disappeared. Durasada also returned home, filled with joy. Whoever reads this hymn will attain all their desires.

Thus ends the thirty-eighth chapter of the Krida Khanda in the Shri Ganesha Purana, describing the attainment of the boon by Durasada.

Chapter 39 : Shiva's Journey to Kashi

The Sage said: Then, the king of demons, intoxicated by the boon he had received from Shiva, could not contain his pride and became completely deluded. Desiring to conquer the earth, he mounted a horse. He had his hair tied up, wore shining earrings, held a sword in his hand, a bow, and was adorned with two quivers. He wore a necklace of pearls and precious gems of great value. With a mark of musk on his forehead, dressed in divine garments, wearing a fine jacket, and his mouth glowing with betel leaf, he was accompanied by two generals. By the strength of their arms, many heroes had been defeated. These two, filled with power, walked on both his sides.

His four-fold army, ready to conquer the earth, covered the sky with dust, appearing as vast as the ocean. He defeated every hero who stood against him. Having seized thousands of jewels and riches, he installed his own trusted and powerful men in those places. Having brought all the kings under his control by assigning them various duties, he became very powerful. Those who were unarmed placed their hands in submission and sought his refuge, while those who were extremely cowardly abandoned their kingdoms and fled. The invincible demon brought even them under his control through his messengers.

Having conquered the globe in this way, he desired to conquer Indra. With his army, he suddenly marched toward the city protected by Indra. Knowing the power of the boon of that wicked one, the King of Gods fled with his family and the gods to a cave. At that very moment, Vishnu left his abode and went to the Ocean of Milk. Madhusudana lay down, placing his head on the lap of Lakshmi. Shiva left the peak of Kailash and went to Kashi. Brahma also went along with him. Wherever anyone fled, the king of demons would install his own powerful and trusted messenger there.

Having thus defeated the gods with sheer pride, the mighty one resided in the land of the fishermen, in the city of Bhasmaka, which is known everywhere in the world as Mukundapura. In that place, there was formerly a powerful king named Bhasmasura, to whom Shiva had given an astonishing boon: "You will die when you place your hand on the head of the one to whom you have given the boon." Driven by his wicked nature, the demon, having received this boon, went to place his hand on the head of the giver of the boon himself. The Lord of the Mountain (Shiva) fled. He was seen by Vishnu, who holds the discus and is all-powerful. Taking the beautiful form of Mohini, he went before him.

He said, "If you will follow my words, O best of men, I shall be your woman." He replied with joy, "Yes." Then she danced, and at her command, he also danced. Just as she showed a gesture, he also showed the same. She placed her hand on her own head, and he did the same instantly. The demon, who was the destroyer of wicked deeds, was reduced to ashes. Mukunda also stayed there, and the city became famous by his name. It grants supreme success immediately to those who perform rituals. That Mohini grants all desires to those who worship her with devotion.

Residing there, the invincible demon ruled the three worlds with pride. He asked his ministers, "How have I conquered the three worlds with my prowess, defeating the enemy of the demons?" Then the ministers said, "You have not conquered everything; there is a tenth part that you have not won. It is incomparable and exists nowhere else. It is where Shiva is served even by all the gods. As long as that city is not conquered, your heroism is in vain." Hearing those words, the lover of war was pleased. He told them, "I shall go there immediately with my army."

Mounting a magnificent chariot, he reached Kashi in an instant. As soon as the demon king entered, there was a cry of distress in the city. The gods who were there all disappeared at that time. Shiva, being a lover of his devotees, did not get angry at all. Saying, "How long is the kingdom given?" he went to the Kedara region with his family. The sages also fled, except for Jaigishavya, who had renounced the region. Then, the invincible demon, possessed by delusion, broke the idols. Having destroyed the great palaces, he rejoiced, being weak in wisdom. If anyone remembered God, he would strike them and cast them out.

There was no Svaha, no Vashatkara, no Svadhakara anywhere. There was no study of the Vedas or the scriptures anywhere. There were no Puranas, no worship of gods, no vows, and no circumambulations. With the wicked-minded Durasada ruling there, the path of duty was erased, and righteousness also perished. With righteousness destroyed, all the gods became hungry.

Thus ends the thirty-ninth chapter, titled "The Tale of Durasada," in the Childhood Deeds of the Playful Section of the Shri Ganesha Purana.

Chapter 40 : The Defeat of Durasada

Gṛtsamada said: Then all the divine sages went to Kedara. They informed the lotus-born Brahma, who was accompanied by Shiva and was the knower of all, about the situation. After bowing their heads, the sages, led by Vāgīndra (Brahma) and Agni, spoke.

The divine sages said: We gods, as well as the sages, have been cast down from our positions. Because of the fear of Durāsada, we are unable to even perform our daily ablutions. Who gave him such a boon that he has become the lord of the world? Why has this wicked one been born, and how can he be killed? Please consider this. Those who are compassionate should do that which brings happiness to the world.

Kṛta (Brahma) said: Hearing their words, the four-faced one spoke. I will tell you the remedy for his destruction, O divine sages. When he performed severe penance for many days, the Lord, the husband of Ambikā (Shiva), granted him boons. He said that there is no god in the three worlds who can kill him. However, that Lord of gods, who divides the three qualities, is the one who created me, Shiva, and Vishnu through his command. Because of the division of the three qualities, he is known as Guṇeśa (Lord of Qualities). He is all-pervading, the master of illusion, the creator and destroyer of the universe, beyond the qualities, the Lord of qualities, and Shiva, who is greater than the greatest. If that Lord takes an incarnation in the womb of Pārvatī, only then will his destruction occur, and not otherwise, O best of gods. Therefore, all of you should praise that Lord who contemplates the universe. First, make the Goddess, the beloved of Shiva, pleased. A child born of her radiance will be the one to kill Durāsada. This is the very boon granted to him by Shankara.

The sage said: Hearing these words from the mouth of Brahma, the gods rejoiced. They praised Bhavānī, the beloved of Shiva, who is affectionate to her devotees.

The divine sages said: We bow to you, the sole cause of the world, the supreme one, the one with wonderful powers in the universe. You are of inconceivable form, worshipped by all the gods, the possessor of the three qualities, and the mistress of the qualities. You are the supporter of the earth, the earth itself, the foundation of all moving and non-moving things. You are the essence of the three worlds, the origin of the three qualities, the embodiment of the Vedas, and the form of the gods. We bow to you, O Goddess, the granter of boons, the enchantress of Vishnu, the giver of positions to the gods. You are the remover of the devotees' distress, the granter of all desires, the creator of the three worlds, and the protector of all things.

Having praised the Goddess in this way, she spoke to the best of the gods: Choose what you desire, O gods, as I am pleased by this hymn. I grant all the desires that are in your minds. Then all the gods told her the story as narrated by Brahma. Then the Goddess told them to praise the Lord of the Ganas (Ganesha), who is the doer of good and evil, the bestower of all success, and the Lord. Having attained all their desires, they are the father of the whole world. Then, all the gods praised Lord Vināyaka with devotion.

The divine sages said: We bow to the remover of obstacles, the compassionate one, the protector of all. You are the cause of the entire world, the all-pervading Lord. You are endowed with many powers, the fulfiller of all desires. You are the god who feels compassion for the poor, the all-knowing, and the ocean of mercy. You take on forms at your own will and are always engaged in various incarnations. You are beyond the qualities, the agitator of the qualities, the teacher of the moving and non-moving, and the Lord. You are the one-tusked, the two-tusked, the three-eyed, and the ten-armed. You have a trunk-like face, you are the destroyer of obstacles and the remover of sins. You are the constant granter of boons to devotees, the creator, sustainer, and destroyer. You have no beginning, middle, or end; you are the origin of beings and the one who makes them grow. You are the Lord of the three worlds, the master of the gods, and the crusher of wicked demons. You have large ears, a brilliant radiance, and wear snakes as ornaments; you are auspicious.

Having praised Lord Ganesha, the bestower of all success, in this way, they heard a voice from the sky: Do not let there be any anxiety in your minds. I will kill the terrible and powerful Durāsada. Hearing this voice from the sky, they returned to Shankara. Seeing him absorbed in meditation, they bowed and spoke to Pārvatī.

The gods said: We do not know who spoke those auspicious words from the sky. Since Hara is absorbed in meditation, what should we do, O Goddess of the universe?

The Goddess said: Then, O gods, the one who is determined now will die. From her, heated by anger and sighing again and again, a supreme radiance emerged from her mouth, nostrils, and eyes. It was filled with garlands of flames, ready to burn the universe. All the gods, with their vision restrained, saw with their divine eyes a great form of Vināyaka, ten-armed, and dispeller of darkness. He wore a crown with the brilliance of ten million suns. He had lightning-like radiance, great jeweled earrings, and auspicious tusks. He wore divine garments, had vermilion, and held ten weapons. He had a musk mark on his forehead and a garland of fruits on his chest. The gods bowed when they realized that his radiance, resembling the fire of dissolution, pervaded the universe and that he wore a snake as a sacred thread.

They then prayed to the Lord, who is the embodiment of bliss: You are seen through supreme yoga; you are beyond reasoning, imperishable, and self-governing. You are free from illness, without appearance, without doubt, ageless, and immortal. You are the form of all, the Lord of all, self-luminous, and the world itself. You are unmanifest, the support of the world, the form of Brahman, and the seer of all things. You are the ancient person, the embodiment of knowledge, and beyond the reach of words. We are blessed, we are blessed! Seeing him, the gods danced.

The sage said: Just as the gods described this radiant Vināyaka, the Mother of the world praised the elephant-faced one. You are the formless, unmanifest, all-pervading, supreme, and beyond qualities. You are reached through meditation, the reflection of consciousness, and the embodiment of existence, consciousness, and bliss. You are all-pervading and the cause of the world, which until today was only contemplated. Today, we see you in a manifest form as Vināyaka, who has descended into my house to kill the demons in many ways. For the benefit of the worlds and to protect the moving and non-moving universe, Pārvatī worshipped him with devotion and gave him a lion as his vehicle. She gave him the name Vakratuṇḍa, which grants all desires to men. She then prayed to him for the destruction of Durāsada and asked for the gods to regain their positions without any rivals.

Thus ends the fortieth chapter of the Krīḍā Khaṇḍa in the Śrī Gaṇeśa Purāṇa, titled "The Incarnation of Vināyaka."

Chapter 41 : The Birth of a Son from Parvati's Energy

Kratu said: Thus praised by the Goddess and the gods, Vinayaka, filled with joy, bowed to Parvati and spoke with affection. Ganesha said: To protect the gods and all the worlds, to destroy the wicked Durāsada, and to remove the burden of the earth, I have descended, O omniscient Mother, to serve you and perform Vedic duties; I shall do whatever you command. As the Lord of Obstacles spoke thus, all the gods, having praised and bowed to Him who is the cause of the causes of the world, said: One who could count the grains of the earth, the stars, or the dewdrops, might perhaps know Your virtues, or one who could measure the water of the ocean. Blessed are the eyes of the gods that have seen the feet of the Lord; now our suffering is gone and we have regained our respective positions. O Lord of all, remove this burden of the earth, this Durāsada.

Kratu said: Hearing the words of the gods, Vinayaka laughed. The Lord, holding ten weapons, said, I shall do everything. Having said this to the gods with a voice choked with joy, the God mounted His lion and quickly went to the city of Varanasi. The hosts of gods, Girija, and Girisha followed Him. Knowing that Vinayaka had arrived, the heroic demon Durāsada, accompanied by his army, emerged from the city. Seeing the demon's army, which was well-equipped with various weapons and roaring like thunder, Vinayaka roared, splitting all the caves. He said to the great demon: Why are you still deluded? You have forcibly defeated the gods, kings, and sages; I was not there then, you wretch. Fearlessly, you have accumulated many sins in the past; now you deserve to receive their fruits from Ganesha. You have oppressed the people and all the inhabitants of the three worlds; for that reason, I have descended to relieve the earth of your burden. Abandon your pride and your stubborn shame and seek refuge; if you go to the battlefield now, you will die. You have tormented the three worlds with the boon received from Shiva.

Having said this to the evil-minded one, Ganesha, eager for battle, lifted His axe, which was adorned with garlands of flames and shone like the fire of dissolution, obscuring the sun in His agitation. He threw the weapon at the demon's heart like the god of death. Struck by the axe, not even a hair on the demon's body moved. With eyes reddened by anger, Durāsada stood up as if to devour the three worlds and said to the God of gods: Neither the gods, nor the King of gods, nor the guardians of the directions have stood before me; how have you, who have come in the form of a child, arrived here now? I do not fear death, you fool; why do you wish to die? Having said this, he drew a brilliant sword from its sheath, with an edge so sharp that mountains would crumble at its strike. The demon struck the God with it, but He warded it off with His goad. The sword was shattered into a hundred pieces by the blow of the axe and fell. With his sword broken, the great demon went for a wrestling match. The God also discarded His weapons and grew in strength. Then a tumultuous and hair-raising battle ensued between them. They struck each other with arms, elbows, and fists. They hit each other with feet, knees, shins, and backs. They leaped and fell upon each other's bodies in turns with great noise, delivering blows with heels, elbows, knees, and shoulders. They rolled on the ground, covered in dust, praising each other and saying, Well done! Seeing the battle continue for many days, the gods were astonished. Feeling ashamed in His heart, knowing the demon's strength, Vinayaka struck a firm blow with His fist on the demon's forehead. His face shattered, and he fell to the ground. He lay there for two muhurtas like a mountain struck by a thunderbolt. He regained consciousness with difficulty after a long time, having lost his senses. He felt himself powerless and was greatly distressed. At the end of the day, Vinayaka returned to the midst of the army. Whoever listens to this battle when the time of war arrives, that mortal shall attain victory just as the God Vinayaka did.

Thus ends the forty-first chapter of the Krida Khanda in the Shri Ganesha Purana, describing the battle between Shri Ganesha and Durāsada.

Chapter 42 : The Victory over Durasada

Gritsamada said: Knowing the prowess of Vakratunda, the king of the demons, gathering his courage, came to fight the elephant-headed god with various weapons. Remembering Shiva and the mantra sacred to the fire god, he invoked an arrow with great devotion and released it at Vinayaka. Fearing the flames of fire, the gods retreated to the heavens.
