In the past, I created beings along with the institution of sacrifice and said: "May this sacrifice be your wish-fulfilling tree." By this, satisfy the gods, and may the gods satisfy you; by mutually satisfying one another, you shall attain the highest and stable state. The gods, satisfied by your sacrifices, will grant you your desired enjoyments; he who enjoys these gifts without offering them in return is indeed a thief. Those who eat what remains after the sacrifice are freed from all sins, but those who cook food only for themselves eat only sin.

Beings are born from food, food is produced by rain, rain comes from sacrifice, and sacrifice is born of prescribed action. Know that prescribed action originates from the Vedas, and the Vedas originate from the Imperishable; therefore, know that the all-pervading Divine is always present in sacrifice. One who does not follow this cycle of the world, living only for the pleasure of the senses, is a base person. However, one who rejoices in the Self, is satisfied in the Self, and is content in the Self has no duty to perform. Such a person has no interest in what is done or not done, and has no dependence on any creature.

Therefore, O King, perform your duty without attachment; by performing actions without attachment, one attains the Supreme. In the past, royal sages like Janaka attained perfection through action; you should also perform your duties for the welfare of the world. Whatever a great person does, others follow; whatever standard they set, the world follows. O King, I have no duty in the three worlds, nor is there anything I have not attained or need to attain, yet I still engage in action. If I did not perform actions, people would follow My path in every way. If I were to cease working, these worlds would perish, and I would be the cause of social chaos and destruction.

Just as the ignorant perform actions out of attachment, the wise should perform them without attachment, for the sake of guiding the world. One should not unsettle the minds of the ignorant who are attached to action; rather, the wise person, being established in yoga, should perform all actions as an offering to Me.

The person of dull intellect, deluded by ego, thinks "I am the doer," while the one who knows the truth of the divisions of the qualities and their functions understands that it is the senses acting upon the objects of the senses, and thus remains unattached. Those deluded by the three qualities of nature are attached to the fruits of their actions; the wise should not disturb them. Therefore, dedicate all your actions to Me, abandoning the sense of "I" and "mine," and you shall attain the supreme goal. Those who follow this teaching of Mine with faith and without envy are liberated from all actions. But know that those who do not follow it, being of distorted mind and envious, are lost and are My enemies.

Even the wise person acts according to their nature; all beings follow their nature, so what can repression accomplish? Attraction and aversion for the objects of the senses are natural; one should not fall under their control, for they are one's enemies. It is better to perform one's own duty imperfectly than to perform another's duty perfectly; it is better to die in one's own duty, for the duty of another is fraught with fear.

Varenya said: O Lord, what is it that compels a person to commit sin, even against their will, as if forced by some power?

Sri Gajanan said: It is desire and anger, born of the qualities of nature, which are the great devourers and the greatest enemies in this world. As fire is covered by smoke, a mirror by dust, and an embryo by the womb, so is knowledge covered by desire. Knowledge is constantly obscured by this insatiable fire of desire, which takes hold of the senses, the mind, and the intellect. Through these, it deludes the embodied soul. Therefore, first control your senses and conquer this sinful destroyer of knowledge and wisdom. The senses are superior to the body, the mind is superior to the senses, the intellect is superior to the mind, and that which is superior to the intellect is the Self. Thus, knowing the Self to be superior to the intellect and restraining the self by the Self, conquer this enemy in the form of desire and attain the supreme state.

Chapter 140: The Yoga of Offering to Brahman

Shri Gajanan said: Long ago, at the beginning of creation, I created the three Gunas and the three bodies, and I imparted this supreme Yoga to Vishnu. Vishnu then taught it to Aryaman, who in turn taught it to his son, Manu. Thus, it was passed down through a lineage and understood by the great sages. Over time, in this final age, it has been lost. O King, it is now disregarded by those lacking faith and is misunderstood by many. I have now revealed to you this ancient Yoga, which I once spoke of; it is the most secret of secrets, the essence of the Vedas, and supremely auspicious.

Varenya said: O Gajanan, you have now descended into a womb and taken birth. How is it that you spoke this supreme Yoga to Vishnu in the past?

Shri Gajanan said: Many births of yours and mine have passed. I remember them all, but you do not have that memory. O mighty-armed one, the gods, including Vishnu, were born from me, and they merge back into me at the time of dissolution in every age. I am the Supreme Brahman, I am the Great Rudra, and I am this entire universe, both the moving and the non-moving. I am the unborn, the imperishable, the soul of all beings, the beginningless Lord. Assuming my Maya of three Gunas, I manifest in many forms. When there is a decline of righteousness and an increase of unrighteousness, I manifest myself to protect the virtuous and destroy the wicked. I eradicate the mass of unrighteousness and establish righteousness. I destroy the wicked demons and perform various divine plays with joy. I protect the social orders, the sages, and the virtuous by assuming various forms. One who knows this divine manifestation of mine in every age, and understands my actions and power in essence, abandons the sense of "mine" and is not born again.

Many have come to me, purified by knowledge and penance, being free from desire, fear, and anger, devoted to me, and taking refuge in me. In whatever way people worship me, I reward them accordingly. Other people, O King, follow my path and conduct their affairs among themselves and others in the same way. Those who desire the fruits of their actions worship the deities and quickly attain the success born of those actions in this world. O King, I have created the four social orders based on the division of Gunas (Sattva, Rajas, Tamas) and actions in this mortal world. Although I am the creator of these, the wise know me as the non-doer, the beginningless, eternal Lord, untouched by the qualities born of action. One who knows me as desireless is not bound by actions. Knowing this, the seekers of liberation in the past performed their duties.

A living being is liberated from all bondage born of ignorance—which is the firm cause of worldly existence—by knowing this truth. I shall now tell you about action and inaction, regarding which even the wise sages have been confused. A seeker of liberation must know the nature of action, inaction, and forbidden action; the paths of these three are very deep. He who sees inaction in action and action in inaction is truly liberated and has accomplished all in this mortal world. The wise call him a sage whose actions are burned by the fire of knowledge, and who performs actions without the seed of desire. Abandoning attachment to the fruits of action, always content and independent, even while engaged in action, he does nothing at all. Free from desire, with a controlled mind, and having abandoned all possessions, he incurs no sin by performing mere bodily actions. He who is free from duality, devoid of envy, equal in success and failure, and content with whatever comes to him, is not bound even while performing actions.

For one who is free from all attachments, established in knowledge, and performs all actions as a sacrifice, his actions dissolve. I am the fire, the oblation, and the offerer; the act of offering is also me. One who is absorbed in Brahman attains Brahman. Some yogis speak of other sacrifices, while others consider the sacrifice itself to be the fire of Brahman. Some offer their senses into the fire of self-control, while others offer objects like sound into the fire of the senses. Others offer all the functions of their senses and life-breath into the fire of self-knowledge, which is kindled by wisdom. Some offer sacrifices through material wealth, penance, or study of the scriptures, while others, who are strictly observant, offer themselves through knowledge. Others, devoted to breath control, offer the outgoing breath into the incoming and the incoming into the outgoing, restraining the paths of both. Thus, all those who are engaged in various sacrifices have their sins destroyed by sacrifice. Those who partake of the nectar of the remnants of sacrifice attain the eternal Brahman. What would this world be for those who do not perform sacrifice?

O King, by knowing these three types of sacrifices—bodily and others—established in the Vedas, you will be liberated from all bondage. Among all sacrifices, the sacrifice of knowledge is considered supreme, for all actions dissolve into knowledge, which is the means of liberation. O tiger among men, this truth should be known by questioning and serving the wise, who are experts in the truth. People who associate with many but not with the virtuous, bind themselves in the world. Through the company of the virtuous, virtues arise and calamities vanish; one attains their own welfare in this world and the next. Other things are easily obtained, O King, but the company of the virtuous is very rare. Having known this, one does not fall into bondage again. Then, one sees all beings in one's own self, and the living being, even if deeply sinful, is liberated from that. Just as a blazing fire turns all fuel to ashes, the fire of knowledge burns all actions to ashes in an instant. There is nothing as holy as knowledge in this world; in time, a yogi attains this within himself through Yoga. One who is full of faith, has mastered the senses, and is devoted to knowledge, attains it and quickly reaches supreme liberation. But the faithless, who lacks devotion and is full of doubt, finds no happiness in this world or the next. Actions do not bind the one who is devoted to self-knowledge, whose doubts are destroyed by knowledge, and who is established in Yoga. Therefore, O King, with the sword of knowledge, cut through the doubt born of ignorance in your heart and become established in Yoga.

Chapter 141 : The Yoga of Renunciation of Action

Varenya said: You have described both the renunciation of actions and the path of Yoga. O Lord, tell me which of these two is definitively superior for my well-being.

Sri Gajanana said: Both the path of action (Karma Yoga) and the path of renunciation (Sannyasa) are means to liberation. Among the two, Karma Yoga is superior to mere renunciation. One who is free from dualities, who bears no hatred, and who desires nothing, is considered a perpetual renunciant, and such a person is easily liberated from bondage.

The foolish and those of little knowledge speak of the renunciation of action and the performance of action as leading to different results, but the wise know that by practicing one, the fruit of both is attained. Whatever fruit is attained through renunciation is also attained through Yoga; he who sees the performance of action and the path of Yoga as one, truly sees.

The wise do not consider the mere abandonment of actions to be true renunciation. A Karma Yogi, performing actions without attachment, becomes Brahman itself. One who is pure, with a controlled mind and senses, devoted to Yoga, and seeing the Self in all beings, is not tainted even while acting. The knower of truth, established in Yoga, does not think "I am the doer," knowing that the eleven senses are merely performing their functions.

One who offers all actions to Brahman and performs them without attachment is not tainted by sin or merit, just as a lotus leaf is not wetted by water. For the purification of the heart, the knowers of Yoga perform actions—physical, verbal, mental, and sensory—while abandoning all desire for the fruits. A man devoid of Yoga performs actions out of desire for fruits and is thus bound by the seeds of karma, leading to suffering.

The Yogi who mentally renounces all actions lives happily, neither doing nor causing to be done, dwelling in the city of the body. I do not create the action, nor the agency, nor the connection between action and its fruit; all is done by the power of nature. The all-pervading Lord does not accept anyone's merit or sin; it is the ignorant, whose intellect is covered by delusion, who are misled. For those whose ignorance is destroyed by the knowledge of the Self, that supreme knowledge shines like the sun.

Those who are absorbed in Me, whose minds are fixed on Me, and who are devoted to Me, have their sins destroyed by this knowledge and attain the state of no return. The wise and noble-minded look with an equal eye upon a learned Brahmin, a cow, an elephant, a dog, or an outcaste. Those who possess this equanimity have conquered the world while still alive, for they have realized the faultless Brahman. Those who are established in Brahman, who are not deluded, and who possess a steady intellect, do not rejoice upon receiving the pleasant nor grieve upon receiving the unpleasant.

Varenya said: O Lord, expert in all knowledge, please tell me what is the greatest happiness in the three worlds, including the realms of gods and Gandharvas.

Ganesh said: One who is absorbed in the Self finds bliss within the Self. That is imperishable happiness, not the pleasure derived from sense objects. Pleasures born of objects are the causes of suffering; they are subject to birth and decay, and the knower of truth does not become attached to them. One who can endure the urges of desire and anger, even when the causes are present, enjoys eternal happiness.

One who is inwardly focused, whose light is within, whose joy is within, and who finds delight within, attains the imperishable Brahman and works for the welfare of all beings. Those who have conquered the six enemies and are masters of their senses see the same Brahman in all.

Sitting in a posture, having withdrawn the senses from external objects, and steadying the gaze between the eyebrows, one should be devoted to Pranayama (breath control). Wise sages describe Pranayama, which arises from the regulation of the incoming and outgoing breath, as three-fold. Know the measure of its practice to be of three types: light, medium, and best. The light Pranayama is said to be of twelve measures, the medium of twenty-four, and the best of thirty-six.

Just as one tames a lion, a tiger, or a wild elephant, one should carefully regulate the breath. When the breath is brought under control, it does not cause harm, and one who has mastered it burns away all sins. As a person climbs a staircase step by step, the knower of Yoga should gradually master the breath. By practicing Puraka (inhalation), Kumbhaka (retention), and Rechaka (exhalation), one becomes a knower of the past and future.

Twelve of the best Pranayamas constitute Dharana (concentration). The Yogi should always practice these two—Yoga and Dharana. O King, one who practices this becomes a knower of the three times (past, present, and future). The three worlds easily come under his control, and he sees the entire world as the form of Brahman within his own Self.

Thus, Yoga and Renunciation yield the same fruit. By knowing Me, the Lord of the three worlds and the giver of the fruits of all actions, one attains liberation.

Chapter 142 : The Fifth Chapter on the Praise of Yoga Practice

Shri Gajanan said: O King, a Yogi who performs Vedic and scriptural duties without desiring their fruits is considered superior to one who abandons action to pursue Yoga. O mighty-armed one, I consider action itself to be the cause for attaining Yoga, while for the perfection of one who has already attained Yoga, tranquility and self-control are the true causes. One who dwells on sense objects becomes his own enemy, but the Yogi who performs his duties without attachment attains perfection. In the matters of friendship and enmity, and in salvation and bondage, the self is its own friend or its own enemy.

The Yogi who is equal in honor and dishonor, in pain and pleasure, toward friends, the virtuous, enemies, the indifferent, those to be hated, and toward both clay and gold, is a self-controlled knower of the truth who has conquered his senses. He should practice Yoga constantly, for such a one is truly the most steadfast. A Yogi devoted to meditation should not practice Yoga when he is exhausted, distressed, agitated, hungry, or when his mind is restless, nor in times of extreme cold or heat, nor in places disturbed by wind, fire, or water. He should avoid practicing in noisy places, dilapidated cow sheds, near fire, near water, on the edge of a well, in cremation grounds, on riverbanks, in places of worship, near anthills, or in areas infested with ghosts. If a Yogi practices in such conditions, he will suffer from loss of memory, muteness, deafness, blindness, fever, or dullness due to ignorance of these defects. A practitioner of Yoga must avoid these faults, for neglecting them will surely lead to such afflictions.

O King, a Yogi who eats too much or too little, who sleeps too much or stays awake too much, cannot attain success in Yoga. By abandoning desires born of imagination, maintaining regulated food and sleep, and controlling the senses with the intellect, one should gradually cease from worldly activities. Wherever the restless and unsteady mind wanders, one should bring it back under the control of the Self through steady determination. By practicing this, the Yogi attains supreme peace, seeing his own Self in the entire universe and the universe within his own Self. I cherish the Yogi who comes to Me through Yoga; I liberate him and never forsake him, and he does not forsake Me. He who sees Me in all beings and all beings in Me, with an equal vision toward pleasure, pain, hatred, hunger, satisfaction, and desire, is a liberated soul. He is a great Yogi, solely united with Me, and is worthy of worship by the gods, including Brahma, in all three worlds.

Varenya said: O Lord, both types of Yoga seem impossible to me, for the inner instrument (the mind) is wicked, restless, and difficult to control.

Shri Gajanan said: One who uses the mind to restrain the mind—which is difficult to control—is like a water-wheel mechanism that frees itself from the cycle of worldly existence. This cycle is firmly bound by the saws of sense objects and secured by the nails of karma, which a person cannot easily cut. The means to conquer this are extreme detachment, indifference to worldly enjoyments, the grace of the Guru, and the company of the virtuous. Through practice, one can subdue the mind for the sake of Yoga; without conquering the mind, O Varenya, Yoga is difficult to attain.

Varenya said: O all-knowing Lord, who holds the wheel of intellect, please dispel my doubt: what is the fate, destination, and result for one who falls from the path of Yoga?

Shri Gajanan said: One who falls from Yoga after attaining a divine body enjoys heavenly pleasures and is later reborn in a family of virtuous and wise Yogis. Due to the impressions of his past actions, he becomes a Yogi again; indeed, one who has performed virtuous deeds never meets a miserable end. Among those devoted to knowledge, penance, and action, the Yogi is superior, and among all Yogis, the one who is devoted to Me is the most excellent.

Thus ends the fifth chapter of the Ganesha Gita, titled "The Praise of Yoga Practice," in the dialogue between Shri Gajanan and Varenya, contained in the Uttara Khanda of the great Ganesha Purana.

Chapter 143 : The Yoga of Intellect

Gajanana said: Understand this truth of Mine with your inner self focused on Me. By knowing this, you will know Me without doubt and will be liberated, for I am the All-Pervading. O King, I shall speak to you for the welfare of the worlds, for there is nothing else to be known, and it is the only means to liberation. First, My nature (Prakriti) must be known, then the object of knowledge, then the attainment of wisdom, and finally, the realization of Me. My nature is elevenfold: fire, water, ether, ego, mind, intellect, air, the sun, the moon, the performer of sacrifices, and the earth. Sages speak of another, higher nature of Mine, which, having become the individual soul, pervades the three worlds. By these two, the entire moving and non-moving universe is created. I am the cause of the world's origin, its preservation, and its dissolution.

Only a rare person, through the merit of past deeds, strives to understand this truth of Mine. Even among those who follow the duties of their caste and stage of life, only a rare soul realizes Me. Such a person sees nothing other than Me and sees everything within Me. I am the fragrance in the earth, the brilliance in fire, the luster in the lotus, and the taste in water. I am the strength of the strong, the intellect, and the power of austerity. I reside in the three-fold modifications (gunas) that arise from Me. Sinners, whose minds are deluded by Maya, do not know Me. My three-fold nature deludes the three worlds. One who knows the truth of My nature abandons all delusion; having known Me through many births, one is liberated.

Others worship various gods and attain them. In whatever way a person worships Me, I fulfill that desire in that very way. I know everything, but no one knows Me. Those deluded by desire do not understand that I, the Unmanifest, have taken a manifest form. I do not reveal Myself to the ignorant and those of sinful deeds. O King, he who remembers Me with faith at the time of death attains the state of no return by My grace. O Ruler of men, whatever deity one remembers with devotion while leaving the body, one attains that state. Therefore, O King, one should remember Me, who possess many forms, day and night. I am the destination for all, just as the ocean is for all rivers.

One who attains the realms of Brahma, Vishnu, Shiva, or Indra may fall again, but he who reaches Me without doubt never falls. O King, for one who worships Me with exclusive devotion, I personally provide for his needs and protect what he possesses. Two paths are ordained, O King: the bright and the dark. By one, one attains the Supreme Brahman; by the other, one enters the cycle of rebirth.

Thus ends the one hundred and forty-third chapter, titled "The Yoga of Intellect," in the Ganesha Gita, which is the essence of the Upanishads and the nectar of Yoga, contained within the Uttara Khanda of the great Ganesha Purana.

Chapter 144 : The Yoga of Worship

Varenya said: O Gajānana, what are the paths known as the bright and the dark? What is the cycle of existence in Brahman? Please be kind enough to explain this to me.

Gajānana said: Fire, light, daytime, the bright fortnight, and the northern path of the sun are the path of the bright. Smoke, nighttime, and the southern path of the sun are the path of the dark. Know that these two paths are the causes of the cycle of existence and the attainment of Brahman. Understand that all that is visible and invisible is Brahman alone. Know that the perishable is five-fold, and within it dwells the imperishable. Know that which transcends both is the pure, eternal reality.

The cycle of existence is described as a series of many births. Those who do not acknowledge me fall into this cycle. Those who worship me properly attain the Supreme Brahman. One should worship me with meditation and other offerings, such as the five nectars, bathing, clothing, ornaments, fragrances, incense, lamps, food offerings, fruits, betel leaves, and monetary gifts. I fulfill the desires of those who worship me with a single-minded heart. My devotee should offer themselves to me with devotion every day. Alternatively, one may perform mental worship with a steady mind, or worship me with effort using fruits, leaves, flowers, roots, and water, and thereby attain the desired fruits.

Among the three types of worship, mental worship is considered the best. Even the worship performed without desire is considered excellent. Whether one is a celibate, a householder, a forest-dweller, or a renunciant, anyone who performs this worship attains perfection. Even one who worships another deity while hating me is, in reality, worshipping me, though improperly, O King. However, one who hates both other deities and me, while worshipping someone else, suffers in hell for a thousand kalpas.

First, perform the purification of the elements and the installation of the vital breaths. Then, withdrawing the activities of the mind, begin the ritual of nyasa. Perform the internal Matrika-nyasa, then the external Shadanga-nyasa, and the nyasa of the root mantra. Then, meditate and chant the mantra. With a steady mind, chant the mantra as received from the mouth of the Guru. Offer the chanting to the Lord and praise Him with various hymns. One who worships me in this way attains imperishable liberation. Shame on the person who is devoid of worship and whose life is in vain.

I am the sacrifice, the medicine, the mantra, the fire, the clarified butter, and the oblation. I am meditation, the object of meditation, praise, the hymn, salutation, devotion, and worship. I am the three Vedas, the object of knowledge, the purifier, the father, and the great-grandfather. I am the Omkara, the sanctifier, the witness, the Lord, the friend, the refuge, and the dissolution. I am the origin, the sustainer, the seed, the shelter, and the abode. I am non-being, death, being, immortality, the Self, and Brahman. Whatever charity, fire ritual, penance, devotion, chanting, or self-study one performs, one should offer all of that unto me.

Women, the wicked, sinners, and those of the three castes are liberated by taking refuge in me; what then to say of the twice-born who are my devotees? My devotee never perishes, knowing these, my manifestations. Neither the gods nor the sages know the origin of my manifestations. I pervade the universe, established through various manifestations. Know that whatever is most excellent in the world is my manifestation.

Chapter 145 : The Vision of the Universal Form

Varenya said: O Lord, Narada has told me of your various manifestations, but I do not know them all, nor does he know them fully. O Elephant-faced Lord, you alone know them all in truth. Now, show me your own vast and beautiful form.

Shri Gajanana said: Behold this entire universe, moving and stationary, within me. You shall see many divine wonders that have never been seen by anyone before. By my own power, I grant you the divine eye of knowledge; how could your physical eyes behold me, the all-pervading, unborn, and imperishable Lord?

Vyasa said: Then King Varenya, endowed with divine vision, beheld the supreme and wondrous form of the Elephant-faced Lord. He saw a form with countless faces, beautiful and vast, with innumerable arms and legs, anointed with divine fragrance, and adorned with celestial ornaments, garments, and garlands. He saw countless eyes and weapons shining like the rays of a billion suns. Within that body, he saw the three worlds in their various forms.

Having seen this supreme divine form, the King bowed down and said: Varenya said: I see in your body all the gods, the hosts of sages, the ancestors, the netherworlds, the oceans, the islands, and the mountains. I see the seven great sages, the earth, the atmosphere, the heavens, humans, serpents, and demons. I see Brahma, Vishnu, Maheshwara, Indra, and all living beings in their various forms. You are beginningless, endless, the source of the worlds, with infinite arms and heads. You shine like a blazing fire, immeasurable and ancient. You wear a crown and earrings, difficult to behold, yet bringing joy.

I see you, O Lord, with a broad chest, being served with great devotion by gods, Vidyadharas, Yakshas, Kinnaras, sages, and humans. I see you being watched with great wonder by dancing Apsaras, singing Gandharvas, and the hosts of Vasus, Rudras, Adityas, Siddhas, and Sadhyas. You are the knower, the imperishable, the one to be known, the protector of Dharma, and the Lord. You pervade the netherworlds, the directions, the heavens, and the earth. Seeing you in this form, both the worlds and I are afraid. O Ganesha, seeing your form with many terrible tusks, your face blazing like the fire of dissolution, and your matted hair touching the sky, I have become bewildered.

All gods, humans, serpents, and the wicked reside in your belly; they emerge from you and enter into you at the end, just as drops of water arise from the ocean. You are Indra, Agni, Yama, Nirriti, Varuna, Vayu, Guhyakesha, Ishana, Soma, and Surya—you are the entire universe. I bow to you, O Lord; please show me grace now. Show me your gentle form that I saw before. Who can know your pastimes, O Infinite One, which you perform by your own will? By your grace, I have seen this divine form, for you have graciously granted me the eye of knowledge.

Shri Gajanana said: O mighty-armed one, even yogis do not see this form of mine; only those like Sanaka and Narada see it by my grace. Those who know the essence of the four Vedas, who are expert in the four Shastras, and who are devoted to sacrifice, charity, and penance do not know my form. I can be seen, known, and entered into only through the feeling of devotion. Cast aside your fear and delusion, and behold me in my gentle form. O King, he who is my devotee, who considers me supreme, who is free from all attachments, who acts for my sake, and who is free from anger and equal toward all beings, attains me.

Thus ends the one hundred and forty-fifth chapter, titled "The Vision of the Universal Form," in the Krida Khanda of the Shri Ganesha Purana.

Chapter 146 : The Yoga of Discrimination between the Field and the Knower of the Field

Varenya said: O Lord, some worship You with exclusive devotion in Your manifest form, while others worship the Imperishable, the Unmanifest. Between these two, who is considered superior? You are the knower of all, the witness, the creator of all beings, and the Supreme Lord. Therefore, I ask You, please tell me, O mighty One.

Shri Gajanana said: Those who serve Me as the manifest form with devotion, fixing their hearts upon Me with exclusive love, are considered the most esteemed by Me. Those who control their senses, work for the welfare of all beings, and meditate upon the Imperishable, Unmanifest, all-pervading, and eternal Brahman—they also reach Me. However, I deliver them from this ocean of worldly existence. The path of worshipping the Unmanifest is fraught with greater difficulty, whereas the path of worshipping the Manifest is easily attainable. Devotion and reverence are the supreme causes for reaching Me; even one who is ignorant but devoted is superior to all scholars.

He who is devoid of devotion is considered an outcaste, even if he is a Brahmin. Conversely, even an outcaste who worships Me with devotion is dearer to Me than a Brahmin. Sages like Shuka and Sanaka, and long-lived beings like Narada, attained liberation and reached Me solely through devotion. Therefore, fix your mind and intellect upon Me with devotion; worship Me, O King, and you shall surely attain Me.

If you are unable to fix your mind steadily upon Me, O King, then strive to reach Me through the practice of Yoga. If you are unable to do even that, then perform all your actions as an offering to Me; by My grace, you will attain supreme peace. If you cannot do this either, then renounce the fruits of all your actions with effort. Knowledge is better than mere practice, meditation is better than knowledge, and the renunciation of the fruits of action is better than meditation, for from renunciation comes supreme peace.

He who is free from ego and the sense of "mine," who is devoid of hatred, compassionate, equal in joy and sorrow, and indifferent to honor and dishonor, is dear to Me. He who does not cause fear in others and is not afraid of the world, who is free from agitation and anxiety, is dear to Me. He who is equal toward friend and foe, indifferent to praise and blame, silent, steady-minded, and unattached, is dear to Me. He who practices this teaching given by Me is worthy of worship in all worlds; he is a liberated soul and is always dear to Me. He who does not hate when encountering the unpleasant, does not rejoice when obtaining the pleasant, and knows the Field and the Knower of the Field—he is most dear to Me.

Varenya said: O Gajanana, what is the Field, who is the Knower of the Field, and what is knowledge? O Ocean of Compassion, please explain this to me as I ask.

Shri Ganesha said: The five great elements, the ego, the intellect, the mind, the five sense organs, the five organs of action, the subtle elements, desire, aversion, happiness, sorrow, the body, and consciousness—this entire aggregate is called the Field. Know Me, O King, to be the Knower of the Field, the indweller of all beings.

Humility, sincerity, service to the Guru, detachment from sense objects, purity, patience, absence of arrogance, reflection on the miseries of birth and death, equanimity, firm devotion, solitude, and self-control—this is called knowledge. Now, listen as I describe the object of knowledge, by knowing which one attains liberation and crosses the ocean of existence.

It is beginningless, devoid of senses, yet the enjoyer of all qualities, though itself beyond them. It is unmanifest, distinct from existence and non-existence, yet it illuminates the objects of the senses. It sustains the universe, is all-pervading, and though one, appears as many. It is full both inside and outside, unattached, beyond darkness, and difficult to know due to its extreme subtlety; yet, it is the illuminator of all that is bright. Know this to be the ancient object of knowledge, the Supreme Brahman, the eternal and supreme Self.

The individual soul, though beyond nature, experiences the qualities born of nature, and these three qualities (Sattva, Rajas, Tamas) bind the soul firmly to the body. When light and patience increase, know that Sattva is dominant. Greed, restlessness, and the desire for action are the signs of Rajas. Delusion, inactivity, and negligence are the signs of Tamas. Sattva leads to happiness and knowledge, Rajas to attachment to action, and Tamas to sleep, laziness, and misery.

O King, humans move toward liberation, worldly life, or lower states based on these qualities. Therefore, be established in Sattva and worship Me with all your heart. With unwavering devotion, know that I reside everywhere—in fire, in the sun, in the moon, and in the stars. Know that the brilliance in a learned Brahmin is also Mine. I create and dissolve the entire universe. I sustain the world through My energy, residing in all senses and as the digestive fire within all beings. I enjoy all experiences, yet I am beyond virtue and sin. I am Vishnu, Rudra, Brahma, Gauri, and the Lord of Ganas. The protectors of the worlds, like Indra, are all born from a portion of My being. In whatever form a devotee worships Me, I reveal Myself to them in that very form. Thus, I have explained to you the Field, the Knower of the Field, knowledge, and the object of knowledge, as you requested.

Chapter 147 : The Yoga of Instruction

Shri Gajanan said: The nature of human beings is of three kinds: Divine, Asuric (demonic), and Rakshasi (monstrous). I shall now briefly describe their signs and fruits. The first leads to liberation, while the other two lead to bondage. Listen as I describe the signs of the first. Freedom from malice, compassion, absence of anger, agility, fortitude, straightforwardness, brilliance, fearlessness, non-violence, forgiveness, purity, and absence of pride—these are the signs of the Divine nature.

Now listen to the signs of the Asuric nature: excessive speech, arrogance, pride, ignorance, and irritability. Cruelty, intoxication, delusion, egoism, and vanity are also signs of the Asuric nature. Hatred, violence, lack of compassion, anger, insolence, impoliteness, performing black magic, and delight in cruel deeds are its characteristics. Distrust in the words of the virtuous, impurity, lack of righteous action, and criticizing the Vedas, devotees, sages, learned Brahmins, and the Smritis and Puranas are also signs. Belief in heretical doctrines and association with impure souls are characteristic of this nature.

Performing deeds with hypocrisy, greed for others' possessions, having numerous desires, constant lying, intolerance of others' success, and obstructing the work of others—these and many others are the qualities of the Rakshasi nature. Those who are devoid of devotion to Me and possess such Rakshasi nature wander between the earth and the heavens. Those who resort to the Tamasic nature surely go to the Raurava hell, where they suffer indescribable pain. Emerging from hell due to destiny, they are born on earth as hunchbacks, the blind, the lame, or in lowly castes. Again, because of their sinful conduct and lack of devotion to Me, they fall.

However, My devotees, even if they are born in any womb, eventually rise. O King, they attain heaven through sacrifices and other righteous deeds. Devotion to Me is rare, while devotion with selfish desires is easy to attain. The deluded, bound by the net of illusion and their own karma, say, "I am the enjoyer, I am the doer, I am the consumer." They think, "I alone am the lord, the ruler, the knower, and the happy one." Such a mindset causes men to fall. Therefore, abandon this and take refuge in the Divine nature. With a firm mind, engage in My devotion incessantly.

That devotion, O King, is also of three kinds: Sattvic, Rajasic, and Tamasic. Devotion to the gods with faith is considered auspicious and Sattvic. Know that devotion which leads to the cycle of birth and death—where one worships Yakshas and Rakshasas with all their heart—is Rajasic. Those who perform cruel, egoistic, and hypocritical acts not prescribed by the Vedas, and who worship ghosts and spirits while satisfying their lustful desires, are Tamasic. By tormenting their own bodies and Me who resides within, they persist in their stubbornness. Such devotion is Tamasic and leads to hell.

Lust, greed, anger, and hypocrisy—these are the four great gates to hell; therefore, one should avoid them.

Thus ends the tenth chapter named "The Yoga of Instruction" in the dialogue between Shri Gajanan and Varenya, contained in the Ganesha Gita, which is the essence of the Upanishads and the nectar of Yoga, within the Uttara Khanda of the great Shri Ganesha Purana.

Chapter 148 : The Yoga of the Actions of the Three Gunas in the Shrimad Ganesha Gita

Shri Gajanan said: O King, penance is also of three kinds, based on the distinctions of the body and other factors. Penance of the body consists of straightforwardness, purity, celibacy, non-violence, the worship of gurus, the wise, and the twice-born, and the constant observance of one's own duty. Penance of speech consists of speaking words that are truthful, pleasant, beneficial, and non-disturbing, as well as the study of the Vedas and scriptures. Penance of the mind consists of inner serenity, gentleness, silence, self-control, and constant purity of heart.

That penance which is performed with faith and without desire is called Sattvic. Penance performed with ostentation, for the sake of reputation, honor, or worship, is called Rajasic. That which is unstable and leads to birth and death is undoubtedly Rajasic. Penance that causes pain to oneself or others is called Tamasic.

Charity given with faith to a worthy recipient at the right time and place is considered Sattvic. Charity given with the expectation of a return, or given with reluctance or effort, is called Rajasic. Charity given at the wrong time or place, to an unworthy person, or with disrespect and contempt, is considered Tamasic.

O King, listen now with a steady mind to the three kinds of knowledge, action, and the doer. That knowledge by which one sees the one imperishable Being in all diverse and perishable beings is called Sattvic. That knowledge by which one perceives diverse and separate entities in all beings is called Rajasic. That knowledge which is irrational, untrue, focused only on the body, and concerned with trivial things is called Tamasic.

Know that actions are also of three kinds. Actions performed as a duty, without desire for fruits, and free from attachment, hatred, or ostentation, are called Sattvic. Actions performed with great effort, driven by desire for fruits, or done out of vanity, are called Rajasic. Actions performed out of ignorance, without considering one's own capacity or the resulting loss, are called Tamasic.

Know also the three kinds of doers. The doer who is steady, enthusiastic, unaffected by success or failure, and free from ego is Sattvic. The doer who is passionate, greedy, violent, impure, and swayed by joy and sorrow is Rajasic. The doer who is unsteady, vulgar, stubborn, deceitful, malicious, lazy, and prone to procrastination is Tamasic.

Hear now of the three kinds of happiness. That which seems like poison at first but ends like nectar, born from the clarity of one's own intellect, is called Sattvic happiness. That which arises from the contact of senses with objects, appearing like nectar at first but ending like poison, is called Rajasic happiness. That which arises from sloth, negligence, and delusion, and which deludes the self from beginning to end, is called Tamasic happiness. There is nothing in the three worlds that is free from these three qualities.

O King, even the Supreme Brahman is described as Om Tat Sat. Everything in the three worlds exists in these three forms. The Brahmins, Kshatriyas, Vaishyas, and Shudras have different duties according to their nature. The duty of a Brahmin is the control of senses, purity, knowledge of scriptures, and the performance of rituals. The duty of a Kshatriya is valor, courage, protection of the refuge-seekers, and governance. The duty of a Vaishya is trade, agriculture, and the protection of cattle. The duty of a Shudra is the service of the twice-born and devotion to the Divine. Those who are devoted to their own duties and offer all their actions to Me attain the supreme state through My grace.

O King, I have thus explained to you this supreme Yoga, with all its parts and details, which has been established since time immemorial. Practice this Yoga that I have revealed to you; do not disclose it to anyone unworthy. Keep it secret, and you will attain the highest perfection.

Varenya said: Having heard these words from the great-souled Ganesha, the King did as he was told. He abandoned his kingdom and family, went to the forest, practiced the Yoga as instructed, and attained liberation. Whoever listens to this supreme Yoga with faith also attains liberation, just like the Yogi. Whoever teaches this Yoga to others with understanding also attains the supreme Nirvana. Whoever studies this Gita, understands its meaning from a guru, and worships Ganesha while reading it daily—whether once, twice, or three times a day—is liberated upon being seen by others, as he becomes one with Brahman.

This supreme Brahman is not attained by sacrifices, vows, charity, fire rituals, study of Vedas, or hearing Puranas, but it is attained by this. Even those who have committed great sins—such as killing a Brahmin, drinking alcohol, theft, or adultery—are all liberated if they read this Gita. Whoever reads it daily with devotion becomes Ganesha himself. Whoever reads it with devotion on the fourth day of the lunar fortnight attains liberation. Whoever bathes in a holy place, worships Ganesha, and reads this Gita with devotion becomes one with Brahman. In the month of Bhadrapada, on the fourth day of the bright fortnight, one who makes an earthen image of the four-armed Ganesha, worships Him according to the rites, and reads this Gita seven times with effort, will be granted all desires by the satisfied Ganesha. He will attain sons, grandsons, wealth, grain, prosperity, knowledge, and happiness, and ultimately, he will attain liberation.

Chapter 149 : The Departure of the Sage

The Sage said: Having accepted the worship in that manner, the Lord resided there, fulfilling the desires of his devotees in that house honored by the King.

The Sage continued: O Brahma, various stories regarding the greatness of Vinayaka have been narrated—how he descended into the house of Varenya and destroyed the demon of obstacles, how he attained the face of Gajasura, and how his manifestation from the womb of Parvati was described. Because he is the elephant-faced one, I had doubts, and there is no one in the three worlds other than you who can dispel these doubts.

The Sage (K) said: O Vyasa, you should not harbor doubts about the Lord who possesses manifold powers. According to the cycle of the Kalpas, he assumes various forms according to his own will. Sometimes Gajanana manifests from the face and anger of Shambhu; sometimes, O Sage, he is born from the radiance and womb of Gauri. Sometimes he is born from the impurities of Gauri; sometimes he is two-faced and two-armed; sometimes five-faced, six-faced, or ten-armed. Sometimes he has twelve hands, and sometimes he is adorned with a thousand arms. In all the scriptures, there are various meditations on him. Do not have any doubt or wonder about this, O Sage. In the Linga Purana, it is described that Brahma and Vishnu were born from Shiva. In the Skanda Purana, the origin of Shiva is described as coming from the eyes of the Creator. Vishnu meditates on Shiva, and Shiva also meditates on Vishnu. The Lord speaks of the five-syllable mantra and the Taraka mantra. The story of Rama, consisting of a hundred crore verses, was divided into three parts. Shankara established the Taraka mantra in the mortal world. Sometimes creation is said to be from Shiva, sometimes from Brahma, and sometimes from Hari. Sometimes it is said to be from the Goddess, sometimes from the Sun, sometimes from Gajanana, and sometimes from the Brahman; all of this is consistent with the scriptures. Whoever harbors doubt about this certainly goes to hell.

The Sage (K) said: Thus, O Sage, the entire story of the Treta Yuga has been told to you. Now, I shall narrate the story pertaining to the Kali Yuga. At the end of the Kali Yuga, O Sage, people will be corrupt and untruthful. Brahmins will be devoid of the Vedas, neglecting their daily baths and prayers. They will never perform sacrifices, priestly duties, or charity. They will accept gifts from the wicked, which is highly improper. People will engage in slandering others, criticizing them, and violating the modesty of women. Everyone will violate the laws in every way. People will certainly betray trust everywhere. They will swear false oaths upon the violation of laws. O great Sage, the clouds will not rain anywhere. People will cultivate crops only on the banks of great rivers. The strong will forcibly take the wealth of the weak. Then, two people will steal from one, and three will forcibly take from those two. Shudras will study the Vedas, and Brahmins will perform the duties of Shudras. Kshatriyas will perform the duties of Vaishyas, and Vaishyas will perform the duties of Shudras. The twice-born will accept gifts from Chandalas. They will become poor, miserable, and confused. They will say, "I have not taken even a bit of wealth," even after having taken it from those who beg. They will accept bribes and give false testimony. They will criticize the virtuous and form friendships only with the wicked. The twice-born will eat meat for no reason. The virtuous will be destroyed, and others will prosper. Forsaking the gods, all people will become slaves to their senses. They will be mentally attached to the worship of ghosts, spirits, and demons. The Brahmins will adopt various disguises and eat anything. Some Kshatriyas will beg for alms in ways unbecoming of their lineage. They will never observe vows or rules. People will perform actions that cause the mixing of castes. In the Kali Yuga, women will be unfaithful to their husbands and break their vows. All people will be like Mlechhas, stealing the wealth of others, merciless, addicted to evil paths, and always devoid of truth. The earth will be devoid of crops, and trees will be devoid of essence. A girl will give birth at the age of five or six. The lifespan will be only sixteen years in that age. Holy places and temples will become hidden.

When sin has grown in this way and Dharma is only a name, the gods will become devoted to fasting. Deprived of offerings like Svaha and Vashatkara, and trembling with fear, they will take refuge in the divine, healthy Gajanana. They will offer various kinds of praise and many bows to him. They will pray to the Lord of Gods, the destroyer of all obstacles. Considering all this, he will manifest as Gajanana, also known as Shurpakarna and Dhumravarna. Mounting a blue horse, holding a sword in his hand, and blazing with anger, he will create an army of various forms according to his own will. He will effortlessly create weapons and great missiles. With his army and his own radiance, he will slay the great Mlechhas. He will kill all the people who have become Mlechhas. He will gather the Brahmins who are living in mountain caves and eating forest food, honor them, and grant them the land. He will make everyone a virtuous person who performs good deeds. Then the world will become full of Dharma, as it was in the Krita Yuga. Having done this, Dhumravarna will then disappear.

Thus, O sinless Vyasa, I have told you the names and forms of the great Vignesha in the four Yugas. His wonderful deeds have been described in every way, by the mere hearing of which all living beings are liberated. I do not have the power to describe his other forms, for even the four Vedas are bewildered by them; what then can be said of me or others like me in describing his greatness? Go now to perform your duties; I shall also attend to mine.

Bhrigu said: O Somakanta, having heard this story from the mouth of Brahma, Vyasa was very pleased and spoke these words that destroy all sins.

The Sage said: I do not find satisfaction even after hearing this most wonderful story. I am blessed and favored that all my doubts have been removed by this conversation regarding the life of Ganesha. Like nectar, I do not get enough of hearing it again and again. By the mere hearing of this, the sins accumulated over many births are destroyed, and all desires are fulfilled. It is only through the power of past merits that people get to hear this. This Purana, which grants all accomplishments, should not be told to the wicked. Having said this, bowing down and circumambulating, Vyasa took leave and went to an excellent forest for penance. There, there were excellent roots, fruits, and water. The wind was not strong, nor was there heat or fire. Sitting there with one-pointed focus, chanting the single-syllable mantra, twelve years passed for the Sage while he was performing penance. Then, Gajanana appeared before him.

Chapter 150 : The Description of the Siddhiksetra and the Blessing of Vyasa

Somakanta said: O Bhrgu, tell me how the Lord appeared before that great soul Vyasa, for hearing this leads to the destruction of sins.

Bhrgu said: O King, listen with respect as I tell you how the elephant-faced Lord appeared before him. He was adorned with red garlands and garments, anointed with red sandalwood paste, possessed an elephant face and lotus-like eyes, and his forehead was radiant with vermilion. He shone like many suns, adorned with divine earrings, armlets, a crown, and bracelets. Seeing Him, the sage closed his eyes, trembled with fear, remembered his mantra, and fainted. Then the elephant-faced Lord said: Do not fear, O best of sages. I am the one whom you meditate upon day and night, and I have come to you. I am the one who is unattainable even by Brahma and others, and I have come to grant you a boon.

Bhrgu said: Hearing this sweet voice, the great sage rejoiced. He placed his head at the Lord's feet and repeatedly exclaimed, "I am blessed, my parents are blessed, my penance is blessed, the earth and the trees are blessed!" He had seen the one who is the embodiment of all, beyond the qualities, the refuge of all, the essence of consciousness and bliss, the infinite, and the cause of all causes. Having praised Him thus, he prayed to the elephant-faced Lord, the giver of boons.

The sage said: Remove all my delusions and grant me firm devotion to You. Enable me to be capable of composing the eighteen Puranas, and grant me the power to praise You. O sinless Lord, if You are pleased with me, dwell in my heart.

Gajanana said: O child, everything you have requested will come to pass today. You will be known as Vyasa, honored by all, the purifier of the pure, possessor of direct knowledge, and the teacher of teachers. You will be renowned for your devotion to Narayana, your virtues, fame, and prosperity. You will be the author of the eighteen Puranas and the same number of Upapuranas, O lord of sages. Because you did not worship me earlier due to pride, nor remember me, you became dumb, but because you heard my glory from the mouth of Brahma, your sins are dissolved. Now, I shall enter your stomach.

Bhrgu said: Having said this, the all-pervading Lord entered the sage's stomach. The sage attained an incomparable radiance equal to ten million suns and illuminated all directions. He then fashioned a magnificent image of Gajanana and installed it in a temple, worshipping it with various offerings. This place became a Siddhiksetra, purifying the eyes of all beings and granting auspicious success in mantras to those who perform spiritual practices.

Having said this and having received permission, Vyasa, who was Narayana himself, narrated the Purana of Ganesha, which he had heard from the mouth of Brahma, to Vaishampayana, and it spread across the earth. O King, I have narrated this entire account to you today. There is nothing in the three worlds as purifying as this, nor anything that grants supreme bliss, O best of kings. O Somakanta, do not tell this to wicked people; it should be told with effort only to the humble and the devout. I constantly chant and remember this; out of compassion, I have narrated it to you, O best of kings. Always remain devoted to the meditation and chanting of Gajanana.

Chapter 151 : The Description of Somakanta Obtaining the Celestial Chariot

Suta said: Having heard the story daily from the best of sages, King Somakanta poured water at the root of the dried mango tree, as per the merit he had acquired. After a year passed, the mango tree, due to the merit of hearing the Puranas, became adorned with sprouts, flowers, and fruits, appearing extremely beautiful. Somakanta, shedding his leprosy, attained a divine radiance. He became free from wounds and, having cast off the foul odor, became fragrant. He was free from sweat, and a divine fragrance spread in all ten directions. Seeing the King with a divine body, radiant as the sun and brilliant as millions of moons, the people were astonished, wondering how such a transformation had occurred. This was the glory of the Ganesha Purana, which destroys all sins.

Bhrigu said: O best of kings, you have attained perfection through the hearing of the Purana. Now go to your son, your ministers, and your people. Seeing them after a year of intense longing, you will experience great joy.

Suta said: Hearing these words, Somakanta fell at the feet of the sage. Immersed in an ocean of bliss, he spoke to Bhrigu. The King said: O sage, today I have witnessed the wonderful glory of your penance. Through the hearing of the Purana, even I, who was fallen and suffering from leprosy, have attained a divine body. You are the creator of this body, O best of sages. Even the sapless mango tree has become filled with fruits and flowers. I do not wish to leave you; what use are my son and people to me? Hearing this, Bhrigu spoke again.

Bhrigu said: It is due to the merit of your past lives that you have heard the Purana from my mouth, by which your sins have been destroyed. Who can describe the glory of the Ganesha Purana? Do not forget me; go now to your city.

As they were speaking, a wonderful, brilliant chariot, radiant as millions of suns, appeared before them. Bhrigu said: O King, this wonderful chariot has come for you. Ascend it and go to the presence of the Lord of the Ganas. That which even the greatest sages cannot attain through rituals, you have obtained from the Lord of the Ganas by hearing this Purana.

As they were speaking, the chariot descended to the earth in an instant. It was accompanied by the attendants of Vinayaka, who were adorned with four arms, crowns, armlets, necklaces, and other ornaments. They were decorated with divine sandalwood, garments, lotuses, and battle-axes. Seeing the Kinnara maidens dancing to the rhythm of drums and the Kinnaras singing, the King spoke again.

The King said: O sage, I had heard many stories about such chariots, but today, through the power of your penance, I see one before me. I have heard of this glory from many mouths, but today I have experienced it myself through your recitation of the Purana.

As the King was speaking, the messengers descended from the chariot, bowed, and spoke to King Somakanta. They said: O pure-souled one, the Supreme God, Gajanana, has remembered you due to your merit and the hearing of the Purana. We, the messengers, have come by His command in this chariot to take you. Go to the supreme abode. By seeing Him, you will be liberated from birth and death, and by His grace, you will attain ultimate happiness in His presence.

Hearing the words of the messengers, the King felt as if he had transcended his body. With his hair standing on end, he spoke in a choked voice: O sinless messengers, have I, the protector of the poor, been remembered by the Supreme God, the creator of the moving and the unmoving, the attributeless one who causes the agitation of qualities, the source of the universe? He whom even Brahma and others do not know, and whom Sanaka and others meditate upon, He, full of infinite qualities, has sent this excellent chariot. Why has He, who takes many forms and many incarnations, remembered me? This is truly astonishing.

Having said this, he bowed to Bhrigu and said: By your command, I shall go with the messengers in this chariot. By your grace and the grace of the Lord, you must not forget me.

Suta said: Hearing his words, Bhrigu, overwhelmed with emotion, with tears of joy and hair standing on end, embraced the King. He said: O King, I am a mere Brahmin, while you are the one who has attained the great fruit. Having become a resident of Ganesha’s city, do not ever forget me.

Having said this, they went outside, holding each other's hands. The King bowed to the chariot and the great sage. Filled with joy and urgency due to the messengers' words, he ascended the chariot. His two ministers, Sudharma and others, also boarded the excellent vehicle. Then the messengers sounded their instruments, filling the directions with music. Somakanta rejoiced at the sight of the chariot's splendor, considering it the result of the merit of his past lives. Bhrigu watched as the chariot ascended into the sky. Seeing that the King had attained the supreme state while still in a mortal body, the sage was filled with great wonder, and after bowing to the messengers, the King departed for that abode.

Thus ends the one hundred and fifty-first chapter of the Krida Khanda of the Shri Ganesha Purana, describing the attainment of the celestial chariot by Somakanta.

Chapter 152 : Somakanta's Journey to the City of the Gods

Suta said: Then the King saw his divine city from above. He rejoiced upon seeing the splendor of the golden gates and mansions. Then, remembering the beautiful assembly, Sudharma, overcome by affection, spoke with a choked voice. She said, "My son Hemakantha must be on the throne, waiting for his parents' return, as a year has now passed. O Lord, treasure of compassion, you should grant me his vision." Hearing these words of the woman, the King lamented.

The King said, "Whether my son Hemakantha is alive or dead, I had told him that I would return in a year. How could I go back on my word? I never spoke an untruth. I am unable to go there or abandon this supreme state. How can this conflict be resolved?" He wept bitterly. Seeing their grief, the messengers said to the noble King, "Hearing your weeping, we have been moved by compassion. We shall take you down for a moment; see him and return quickly. When you are satisfied, we shall be greatly pleased." Then the messengers brought them down to the north of the divine city.

That chariot of Ganesha, having arrived for the hearing of the Puranas, filled the city with radiance and the sound of musical instruments. Subala and Jnanagamya, having bowed to the King and the attendants, went to tell Hemakantha the whole story. In an instant, they saw Hemakantha on the throne, surrounded by brave warriors and ministers, watching a magnificent dance. The ministers saw Subala and Jnanagamya. All the warriors and Hemakantha stood up. They rushed to embrace them with great excitement.

After the embrace, the King's son, filled with great joy and with hair standing on end, said to them, "Are my mother, father, and ministers safe? Why did you leave my parents and come alone?" Having said this, he seated them on thrones and honored them with clothes, sandalwood, ornaments, fruits, betel leaves, and gold. "Without them, my life hangs by a thread. Day and night, I think only of them, no one else. My father once said he would return; how could he break his word?"

They replied, "Do not worry in vain, O King; your parents are well. Who can know the power of their merit in the three worlds? We left the city with your permission. Your father suffered greatly due to his delicate nature. He was tormented by hunger, and his lotus-like feet were bleeding. He found no satisfaction in roots and fruits. Your mother was in a similar state and could not take a step. After wandering, they saw a beautiful, large lake. The King slept there, cooled by trees and vines, while Sudharma, exhausted, massaged his feet. We went in search of roots and fruits, and there we met the sage Chyavana, who had come for water. He took them to his hermitage, sensing their plight. We arrived there with roots and fruits. We all went to the hermitage, were honored by the sage, and were fed with six-flavored food, finding supreme rest.

Then a conversation took place between the sage and the King, and the King told the sage the whole story. The sage, through the power of meditation, realized the King's previous birth and, full of compassion, told him the remedy for his sins. Upon hearing of his sins, the King was terrified. While he was in doubt, a white bird emerged from his body and began to devour the King. Distressed by the pecking of its beak, the King fell. He prayed to the sage for protection, and at the mere sight of the sage, the birds vanished. The King stood with folded hands, and realizing the greatness of his sins, he meditated with devotion.

The compassionate sage had the Ganesha Purana recited for a year, preceded by the chanting of the divine name one hundred and eight times. Through the power of his penance, he extracted a terrible, hungry, and disheveled man from the King's body. The sage told him to eat a large, dry mango tree. The tree touched by him turned to ash instantly. Then the sage told him to eat the fisherman. Then the sage told the King to offer his merits daily into those ashes while listening to the Purana. "As soon as this mango tree becomes as it was before, you too, O King, will attain a divine body."

The King did exactly that; he bathed, listened to the stories, and daily offered his merits into the ashes with devotion. At the end of the year, when the Ganesha Purana was completed, the tree became full of fruits and flowers as before. The King also attained a divine radiance, shining like the sun and moon. While the King was praising the sage, the chariot arrived, accompanied by music and dance, filled with Ganesha's messengers, and stood near the hermitage. O Lord, our eyes were blessed to see that splendor. By the grace of God, the messengers of your father, prompted by the sight of the King, placed us in the chariot. Sudharma, having received the sage's permission, traveled in the chariot. As soon as we saw the divine city, we remembered you. Therefore, the chariot was brought down to the north of the city. Your parents have gone to inform you of their arrival. Go quickly to see them, or else they will leave."

Hearing these words, Hemakantha, filled with haste, tears, and joy, ran to see them. With his ornaments scattered and surrounded by citizens and servants, he reached the chariot filled with attendants in an instant, his eyes streaming with tears.

Thus ends the one hundred and fifty-second chapter of the Krida Khanda in the glorious Ganesha Purana, titled "The Vision of Hemakantha."

Chapter 153 : The Description of Somakanta Attaining the Abode of the Divine

Suta said: The four ministers went before Somakanta and, bowing down, informed him that his son had arrived. Just as Indra is surrounded by gods, the prince was surrounded by all the warriors and citizens. Seeing the divine chariot, the king was filled with both joy and sorrow. Informed by his ministers, the king arrived quickly, accompanied by children, women, the elderly, and his servants. Seeing the splendor of the chariot, everyone was filled with joy, and upon seeing Somakanta within it, they bowed to the ground. He was accompanied by the messengers of Ganesha and his beautiful wife. Stepping down from the chariot, he embraced his son with joy. Both shed tears of happiness, their bodies trembling with emotion; they stood like statues, unable to speak for a moment.

Then the father spoke to his son, who had returned after a year, with great affection: "I always meditated on you, my virtuous son, the ornament of our lineage. I see no one among all the people who compares to you." Then Sudharma, filled with love, ran to her son and embraced him, both of them shedding tears. The mother said, "My child, I have seen you after a long time. My heart was filled with longing for you, and I found no happiness. Now, seeing your face, my mind is filled with joy. Because of your separation, my body became very frail." Then the citizens and the king's subjects embraced him. Some circumambulated him, some touched his feet, and some returned to the city just by having seen him.

Then the king addressed everyone: "By the grace of Bhrigu, I heard the Ganesha Purana, which destroys all sins. Through it, I became sinless and attained a divine body and this chariot. I have seen everyone, and now I shall return in this chariot. By the grace of the merciful Ganesha, this chariot was sent to me on earth. I have enjoyed the kingdom and its wealth according to my desire. Now, by the grace of Bhrigu, I shall go to the supreme abode." Hearing these words spoken by Somakanta, everyone wept loudly, and some fell to the ground. The leaders of the guilds then said to the merciful Somakanta, "O King, how can you go to that abode without us? We will all abandon our lives and follow you. If you leave, the sin of our deaths will be upon your head. We have no merit by which we could see that Lord of Virtues. By your grace, we shall go to that supreme world. There is no happiness in this world; life is wasted here. We have no other means to reach that divine abode."

Then Hemakantha respectfully said to his father, "Father, how can you leave your child behind to go and see Gajanan? What is the use of the kingdom to me? For a year, I have ruled the entire kingdom by your command, and now I have no desire for it. I swear by you, O King, you must take me with you." Somakanta replied, "Everyone desires to see the lotus feet of the Lord, and I am dependent on the Lord's will; what can I do, O people? I grieve because of my affection for you, especially my love for my son. That is why I brought the chariot down from the heavens to see you."

Suta said: As the people grieved and Somakanta wept, the divine messengers felt compassion. They said to Somakanta, "O King, you are blessed, for these people are as devoted to you as you are to the Lord of the World. Therefore, take everyone and we shall quickly go to Gajanan." The king said, "If the entire city is taken, then my fame will be firmly established on earth; otherwise, it will not be so."

Suta said: Having said this, the king, by their command, gave the holy water and the merit born from hearing the Ganesha Purana into their hands. As the water touched their hands, the people became sinless and radiant with merit. Children, women, and the elderly all climbed into the excellent chariot. Then the messengers of Ganesha ascended into the sky. Some sinful people who did not receive the water in their hands returned to the city and told the remaining people what had happened. Then everyone saw the city rising into the sky. Those who remained spoke to one another, satisfied by the gain of wealth. They became engaged in various businesses with happy hearts. The messengers, wielding staffs, caught those who tried to flee and, out of curiosity, threw them into the chariot by force. Those who had gone under various pretexts were also cast in. The queen, the children, the ministers, and the citizens sat in the middle and entered happily, chanting, "Mayuresha, Mayuresha, victory to you!" As divine instruments played and the Apsaras danced, the echoes roared through the directions. With great speed, they reached the auspicious abode of Ganesha. Seeing the splendor, they exclaimed in wonder, "Oh, great is the merit of him by whom Gajanan is seen!" Everyone approached, and the king attained union with the Lord.

Suta said: Thus, O Brahmins, I have told you the Ganesha Purana, which destroys the sins of all who hear it. Listen now to the account of what the king asked the divine messengers while in the chariot; I shall tell you that story as well.

Thus ends the one hundred and fifty-third chapter of the Krida Khanda of the Shri Ganesha Purana, titled "The Description of Somakanta Attaining the Abode of the Divine."

Chapter 154 : Vinayaka in Varanasi

The sages said: O sinless one, what was asked by Somakanta while he was in the celestial chariot? We wish to hear all of that; please tell us everything in its entirety.

Suta said: While traveling through the aerial path, the messengers were asked by the noble-minded king about the names of the great Vinayakas residing in Varanasi and their attendants. Listen to them, O sinless ones.

The King said: O messengers, please tell me in full about the Vinayakas who are in the entourage of Vishvesha (Lord of the Universe), as remembering them grants all accomplishments.

The messengers said: O King, we shall tell you briefly about the Vinayakas situated in the enclosures of Vishvesha. Listen to them in order, as they remove all fears. Durga Vinayaka, Arka Vinayaka, Bhimachandi Vinayaka, Dehali Ganapa, and Uddanda Vinayaka. Pashapani Vinayaka and the Vinayaka who removes all obstacles are in the first enclosure. Siddhi-Siddhirupa Vinayaka, Lambodara, Kutadanta, and Shulatanka Vinayaka. Kushmanda is the fourth, the fifth is known as Munda, then Vikata-dvija, Rajaputra Vinayaka, and Pranava are situated in the second enclosure. Vakratunda, Ekadanta, Trimukha Vinayaka, Vighnaraja is the fourth, then Varada Vinayaka, Panchasya, Heramba Vinayaka, and Modakapriya are situated in the third enclosure. Vinayaka, Abhayaprada, Simhatunda Vinayaka, Kunitaksha, Kshipra-prasada, the famous Chintamani, Dantahasta Vinayaka, and Prachanda are the eight Vinayakas to be known in the fourth enclosure. Uddanda-Munda Vinayaka, Sthuladanta, the second is Kalapriya Vinayaka, Chaturdanta, Dvitunda, Jyeshtha Gaja Vinayaka, Kala, and Nagesha are the eight Vinayakas to be known in the fifth enclosure. Manikarna Vinayaka, Ashasrishti Vinayaka, Yaksha, Gajakarna, Chitraghanta Vinayaka, Sumangala, and Mitra are the Vinayakas in the sixth enclosure. Moda, Pramoda, Sumukha, Durmukha Vinayaka, Ganapa, and Jnana Vinayaka are to be known in the seventh enclosure, along with Dvara Vinayaka. Avimukta is the eighth, where the Vinayaka who grants liberation resides. Others are Bhagiratha, Harishchandra Vinayaka, Kapardi, and Bindu Vinayaka. The daily remembrance of these grants the fruits of all desires.

Whoever rises in the morning and reads these with a pure mind, obstacles will not hinder any of his actions. O King of kings, a devoted man should worship them with each name using durva grass, rice grains, sesame seeds, and shami leaves. He will accomplish the impossible, be victorious everywhere, and attain longevity, nourishment, health, and excellent wealth. Everything you asked, O King, has been told to you. The divine messengers then happily returned to their own abode.

Suta said: O sages, I have told you about the enclosures of Vishvesha. Now, I shall narrate the fruits of hearing this Purana.

Thus ends the one hundred and fifty-fourth chapter of the Krida Khanda of the Shri Ganesha Purana, titled The Description of the Fifty-Six Vinayakas.

Chapter 155 : The Fruit of Hearing (Phalashruti)

Suta said: O Brahmanas, by taking refuge in this Purana, birth and death are averted. If one listens to it, whether once or many times, even Shesha or Brahma would be unable to fully describe its glory. A person who, with devotion, gives thousands of loads of gold to Brahmanas during solar or lunar eclipses at Kurukshetra attains the same merit by listening to this Purana. Who can weigh the merit of listening to this Purana against the fruits of all the sacrifices performed with proper offerings? Therefore, the merit of listening to this Purana is exceptional; how can one even calculate the value of vows and water-offerings in comparison? The merit gained from listening to this Purana is ten million times greater than that of giving away ten million maidens or thousands of cows. It is also ten million times more meritorious than the constant study of the four Vedas with their limbs, the study of scriptures, or the service of the righteous. The merit gained from listening to the entire Ganesha Purana is ten million times greater than any other virtuous act.

In the house where the Ganesha Purana is written, no demons, ghosts, spirits, or malevolent entities can enter. Planets and childhood afflictions never cause any suffering there, for that house is always protected by Lord Ganesha Himself. A person who listens to or worships this Purana with a focused mind becomes purified just by their presence. Such a person becomes respected by Brahma and other sages. If Ganesha is angry, He can burn the worlds, but if pleased, He grants the highest position. By listening to this daily, a person attains the eight siddhis (perfections) and never encounters poverty or distress. They obtain their desired objects and even the divine treasures. The wish-fulfilling tree, the Kamadhenu cow, and the Chintamani jewel come under their control, and they become worthy of worship. The arts of subduing, destroying, paralyzing, or driving away enemies are nullified in an instant just by remembering this Purana.

A person who listens to this Purana while sitting near Ganesha is freed from great sins, including the killing of women or children, and undoubtedly attains the proximity of Ganesha at the end of life. Even a Shudra, by having Brahmanas sit in their midst and listening to this, gradually attains the status of a Vaishya, Kshatriya, or Brahmana. One who has neglected their daily or occasional duties attains the perfection of those duties through the grace of Ganesha. On the fourth day of the bright fortnight of Bhadrapada, one who installs an earthen idol, worships it with great devotion in a beautiful pavilion, and listens to the Purana, will find Vinayaka pleased. He grants all desires and, in the end, liberation. If a person reads the hymns contained herein daily with devotion, they undoubtedly attain perfection. By following the prescribed rituals and chanting for a month, and feeding Brahmanas with devotion, one becomes one with the Divine.

The sages asked: O sinless one, O son of Suta, tell us who attained merit by listening to this in the past. Suta replied: Listen, O sages. Once, a mute sage named Lomasha went to Brahmaloka by chance. After bowing to the Lord of the worlds and sitting by His command, he was worshipped with great devotion. Lomasha then asked Brahma: O Lord, please tell me the merit-increasing Ganesha Purana that was narrated to Vyasa. Brahma replied: Listen carefully, O Lomasha, to this auspicious Purana of Ganesha, which destroys all sins and grants both worldly desires and liberation to mankind. Then, Brahma narrated the Ganesha Purana to Lomasha. The mute sage, greedy for the divine sound, listened to it all with devotion. Thereafter, he spoke as eloquently as the Lord of Speech, studied it, taught it, and made others listen. After enjoying worldly pleasures and having children and grandchildren, he finally attained the supreme abode of Ganesha.

Listen again, O sages, to an ancient history. There was a king named Samvarana, born in the Ikshvaku dynasty, who was pure, righteous, a performer of sacrifices, a giver of gifts, and a conqueror of enemies. He was a protector of all subjects and respected in the three worlds. Being childless, he performed the Putrakameshti sacrifice, but still did not get a son. He then listened to the Harivamsha, but still no son was born. By divine providence, a famous sage who knew the Ganesha Purana arrived at his house. King Samvarana requested him to stay, and after listening to the Ganesha Purana from his mouth, the king was overjoyed. He honored the sage with jewels, pearls, clothes, and gold. Soon after, he was blessed with a son and became devoted to Ganesha, eventually attaining the supreme state.

His sister was barren for thirty years, but after listening to the Ganesha Purana, she was blessed with a son. She then became devoted to Ganesha, enjoyed worldly pleasures, and finally attained His divine abode. Even the son of Sagara, who was lame, listened to this holy Purana from Lomasha for twelve years with humility and devotion. By satisfying Lomasha with gifts, he regained his health and attained victory, longevity, and the divine abode. The merit one gains from listening to all eighteen Puranas is attained by listening to this one Ganesha Purana. A woman who is barren or suffers from miscarriages will, by listening to this, bear healthy children. A mute person gains eloquence, and a Brahmana becomes well-versed in the Vedas. A Shudra becomes a Vaishya, a Vaishya a Kshatriya, and a Kshatriya a Brahmana just by remembering this. A maiden who listens to even one chapter of this Purana gains a husband who is virtuous, wealthy, and of noble lineage. A person born blind gains sight by taking refuge in this Purana.

The merit that one gains by bathing in all holy rivers and giving all kinds of gifts is attained by one who listens to this with devotion. The merit of performing the five-fire penance in summer, staying in water in winter, or living under the open sky during the rainy season for many years is attained by listening to just five chapters. The merit of one who constantly serves the Agnihotra with devotion is attained by listening to this Purana. The merit of one who stands on one toe for ten thousand years is attained by listening to ten chapters. There is no doubt that a person who listens to this throughout their life becomes a universal monarch. The merit of living in Kashi or bathing at Prayaga for a thousand Magha months is attained by listening to this Purana. One who listens to this with devotion has no fear of weapons or enemies. I have narrated this entire Purana to you in detail. Its complete glory cannot be described even in ten million years by Brahma, Shanmukha, or Shesha. O sages, you have asked about that which destroys all sins, grants all desires, provides both enjoyment and liberation, and increases merit. What else do you wish to hear about the Lord Ganesha, who performs various divine plays and removes the sins of both the speaker and the listener?

Thus ends the 155th chapter of the Krida Khanda of the Ganesha Purana, describing the fruits of listening to the Purana.

Thus ends the Ganesha Mahapurana.
